# YouCoded Roadmap

Single planning surface for the whole product (app + registries + worker + plugins).
Format: checkbox items with backtick tokens — type (`bug`|`feature`|`idea`, default `feature`),
milestone (`vX.Y.Z`), tags (`#kebab-case`, same vocabulary as custom session tags),
issue link (`repo#N`), `(added YYYY-MM-DD)`. Section headers pass their tokens down;
an item's own tokens win. Unknown tokens degrade to tags. `[x]` = shipped (note the
commit/PR in the detail line) — shipped items collect in ## Shipped.
Rolling cleanup: at each release, move ## Shipped entries older than the previous
release to `docs/archive/roadmap-shipped.md` — the ROADMAP is a live planning
surface, not a history.

- [x] Native-session safety fixes (the v1.3 gate) `bug` `#native-runtime` `#sync` (added 2026-07-18, **SHIPPED 2026-07-19 — youcoded#177, merge `fe8529ba`**)
  **Descoped from full parity by Destin on 2026-07-18** after the design review found that cross-device native *resume* is blocked by a design question the plumbing can't answer (see the v1.3.1 item). v1.3 ships correctness only — three fixes, all independently reviewable:
  1. **Orphaned harness (investigation Break 4).** `nativeHost.resume()` never destroys an existing live entry, and `takeover.ts` + `session-exit` skip `nativeHost.destroy` entirely — so two `HarnessSession`s can append to one native JSONL, violating the single-writer invariant at `native-home.ts:5-7`. Data corruption; the load-bearing fix is in `resume()`, not the callers.
  2. **Phantom `claude/` records (found 2026-07-18 in review, NOT in the investigation).** `SESSION_SET_FLAG`'s phantom-record gate (`ipc-handlers.ts:2374`) keys off `sessionIdMap.has()`; PR #176's `sessionIdMap.set` for native defeated it, so flagging/noting a live native session seeds a mislabeled `provider:'claude'` record with blank metadata that syncs everywhere and is never pruned. **Confirmed on disk** (`~/YouCoded/Personal/Conversations/claude/e0a23b35-….json`). Needs the gate fixed *and* a cleanup pass for records already written.
  3. **Gate the native lease acquire** (investigation Option A) so the app stops offering a takeover it cannot perform.
  Spec: docs/archive/specs/2026-07-18-native-sync-parity-design.md (archived shipped 2026-07-23 — its §§4-6 parity work landed in v1.3.0 via M2, not v1.3.1).

- [ ] Project-scoped skills in native sessions — `<cwd>/.claude/skills/` is never discovered `feature` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-08-05)
  Claude Code auto-discovers a repo's own `.claude/skills/`; the native harness does not. `scanSkills()` (`desktop/src/main/skill-scanner.ts:21`) takes no arguments and all three of its passes are home-scoped, so a native session opened on a repo cannot use that repo's skills and the drawer under-reports what a CC session in the same folder already runs. **Commands already have the pass** — `command-provider.ts:71-72` scans `<cwd>/.claude/commands/` — so the precedent exists; skills just never got it. Design settled with Destin 2026-08-05: docs/active/specs/2026-08-05-project-scoped-skills-design.md. Shape: a separate `scanProjectSkills()` (NOT a fourth pass — `scanSkills()` must stay home-scoped or `LocalSkillProvider.installedCache` goes wrong), precedence plugin > project > user matching the command side, shadowing annotated in the drawer rather than silent, auto-load with attribution and no trust gate (the permission engine is the boundary and does not move), and a per-session catalog in `NativeSessionHost` keyed by cwd.
  **Carries a real bug fix:** `main.ts:194-197` resolves the project cwd for commands as `sessions[0]?.cwd ?? null` — the FIRST session, not the active one — so with two sessions open on different folders, project *commands* already come from the wrong folder today. Main has no active-session concept (`session-browser.ts` only takes an `activeSessionIds` set), so `cwd` becomes an argument on both `skills:list` and `commands:list` and the renderer, which knows, supplies it.
  **Android deferred by Destin 2026-08-05**, and two pre-existing Android gaps go with it: `app/.../skills/SkillScanner.kt` has no project pass, and it has no `~/.claude/skills/` (user-authored) pass at all — it stops after the two plugin passes, so it does NOT mirror the desktop scanner despite being described that way. Both belong to M8.
- [ ] Per-project description — synced, user-written label on the project card and list `feature` `#sync` `#ui` (added 2026-08-05)
  Design settled with Destin 2026-08-05: docs/active/specs/2026-08-05-project-description-design.md. A **label for the user, not agent context** — it never enters a session's prompt. Rides the existing project-registry machinery next to `displayName`, so the synced half is mostly repetition of a shipped pattern. UI approved in the workbench (18 numbered changes, branch `feat/project-description`): description under the path row in italic curly quotes with inline edit, a third truncated line on switcher rows, and the sync status strip compressed into a **pill that opens its own popover** carrying every state's full copy plus its action — including "Stop syncing", which moved off the actions row.
  **Two traps that are easy to get silently wrong, both verified against source.** (1) Do NOT bump `PROJECT_REGISTRY_SCHEMA`: `parseEntry` rejects on strict inequality and reads are fail-soft-skip, so bumping it makes every older device drop *every* record — projects vanish from discovery/rename/stop until that device updates. Add the field tolerantly at schema 1. (2) Do NOT reuse `updatedAt`: `mergeProjectEntries` picks the newer entry wholesale, so a shared clock means writing a description on one device silently reverts a rename made on another. It needs its own `descriptionUpdatedAt` and an independent LWW join. A known residual hazard has no fix from the new side: the existing setters rebuild entries from explicit fields rather than spreading `cur`, so an older device that renames or stops a project drops the description for everyone — mixed-version window only, and worth fixing the setters so the *next* field added survives it.
  **Android is not free, and the two channels differ** (verified in `SessionService.kt`): `syncspaces:rename-project` is a fast-reject `not-implemented-on-mobile` stub at :3738, so `syncspaces:set-project-description` must join that list or the editor hangs on a phone; but `folders:rename` at :1315 is a *real* Kotlin implementation, so `folders:set-description` needs a real one to match. Open questions in spec §8: switcher rows still use status dots while the card now uses glyphs (`sync-spaces.md` pins the dots), card wraps vs list truncates, and the narrow cog's sync entries are now duplicated by the popover.
- [ ] App-native hover tooltips — replace browser `title=` on the surfaces users actually watch `feature` `#ui` (added 2026-07-28)
  Destin 2026-07-28, on the new /clear "Cleared — still here to read, but not in Claude's context" hint: browser-default tooltips look foreign to the app. **This REVERSES a documented prior decision** — `components/ui/AnchorTip.tsx`'s header states the policy explicitly ("Native `title=` hover hints are NOT this component — they stay as-is (~231 across 63 files). AnchorTip is for rich/click-open info; `title` is for plain hover hints. Two tools, one documented policy."). Re-verified 2026-08-12: **267** `title=` sites across 82 files in `src/renderer/` (was 245 on 2026-07-28), heaviest in SettingsPanel (27), StatusBar / SessionDrawer / HeaderBar (8 each). **AnchorTip cannot be reused as-is** — it renders its OWN (i) glyph as the trigger, so it is an info-button, not a wrapper you can put around a chip or a chat bubble; the new thing is a `<Tooltip>` that wraps arbitrary children. It should reuse AnchorTip's hard parts, which are already solved there: portal + the L4 Overlay layer (so it is not trapped behind the panel it describes), capture-phase reposition on scroll/resize, and Esc via the shared `useEscClose` LIFO stack. **Scope recommendation: NOT all 245.** Migrating everything is ~a day plus a visual pass, and each swap turns an attribute into a wrapper element, which can perturb flex/grid — status-bar chips and header buttons especially. Do the surfaces in constant view (status bar, header bar, chat timeline) and leave settings/marketplace on `title` until touched anyway. **Genuine tradeoff to preserve, not just work:** `title=` is free accessibility, cannot overflow or be clipped, and survives the window losing focus — a custom tooltip has to earn all three back, and these triggers are dense (the status bar sits at the screen edge; the timeline scrolls). Deliberately NOT bundled into the native-runtime branch: a 63-file migration inside a 50-commit branch would have made its review materially harder.

  **Phase 1 (index + read-only CLI) MERGED 2026-08-06** — youcoded#282 + #283 (transcript-resolution fix), wecoded-marketplace#65 + #66. Still **never exercised by a human**: the checklist and the traps are in `docs/active/handoffs/2026-08-10-chatsearch-state-of-play.md`, and recall quality should be tested before more is built on top. A separate follow-on design (Preview/Resume from a search hit) is specced but unbuilt: `docs/active/specs/2026-08-10-chatsearch-session-references-design.md`. Atomic-rename claims, since the live app and every dev instance share `~/.youcoded/`. **Phase 3** = per-work-item digests (`resolved | open | abandoned | unclear`) behind an off-by-default Preferences toggle and a model picker, on the four lazy triggers. Note phase 3 unblocks two things phase 1 ships deliberately inert: the `○` open marker, and `--state open`, which currently answers "cannot be determined yet" rather than returning zero results. - [ ] A newly bundled plugin cannot install for up to 24h after publishing `bug` `#registries` (added 2026-08-10)
  `LocalSkillProvider.fetchIndex` caches the marketplace index at `~/.claude/youcoded-marketplace-cache/index.json` with a 24h TTL (`INDEX_TTL`, `skill-provider.ts`). `ensureBundledPluginsInstalled` → `installMany` → `install(id)` looks the id up in **that cached index**, so between publishing a plugin and the cache expiring, a bundled plugin is silently not installed — the entry is in `BUNDLED_PLUGIN_IDS`, merged to the registry, and absent from `installed_plugins.json`, with no error anywhere. Hit for real with `youcoded-chatsearch` on 2026-08-10: the cache held a 174-entry snapshot fetched 17.7h earlier while the live registry served 327. Workaround = delete that one file and relaunch. Note `installFromLocal` has its own **1h** cache repo refresh, so only the index lookup is the slow gate; the two TTLs disagreeing is itself worth a look. Candidate fixes: bypass/ignore the TTL for ids in `BUNDLED_PLUGIN_IDS`, force a refetch when a bundled id is missing from the cached index, or shorten `INDEX_TTL`. Low user impact (self-heals within a day) but it makes bundled-plugin rollout untestable on the day you ship it.
- [x] Fresh `npm install` leaves the desktop dev app unlaunchable `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-06, **SHIPPED 2026-08-11 — youcoded `979e08e3`: desktop/package.json now carries the allowScripts block (electron true; node-pty/koffi/electron-winstaller explicitly declined), so a clean clone downloads the Electron binary and run-dev.sh comes up. Verified against the origin/master blob 2026-08-12.**)
  Hit while dev-testing chatsearch. `desktop/package.json` on master had no `allowScripts` block, so npm blocked electron's postinstall and the binary was never downloaded — `run-dev.sh` then dies with "Electron failed to install correctly" while Vite still comes up, which makes it look like a partial start rather than a missing dependency. It had been papered over by an **uncommitted local** `allowScripts` block in the main checkout, which pulling master removed. Workaround used: `node node_modules/electron/install.js`. Real fix = commit the `allowScripts` block (electron, node-pty, koffi, electron-winstaller) so a clean clone works, or document the approve step in setup.
- [x] Transcript-path validators do prefix checks without a trailing separator `bug` `#tech-debt` (added 2026-08-12, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #294, merge `cd1d94ac`: both stragglers now append `path.sep`, with mutation-verified tests driving the real IPC handler and WS frame. Review found the whole `claudeProjects` family is now consistent (4/4 sites).**)
  Noticed during the slug-encoding spec verification (not part of that fix's scope): `ipc-handlers.ts:929` and `remote-server.ts:1658` validate a caller-supplied transcript path with `resolved.startsWith(claudeProjects)` and no `path.sep`, so a sibling directory like `~/.claude/projects-evil/…` passes the containment check. The other three validators of the same family (`ipc-handlers.ts:1057-1059`, `remote-server.ts:1683-1685`) already append `path.sep` — align the two stragglers. Low severity (paths come from our own UI, not untrusted input), but it's a two-line fix and the inconsistency invites copy-paste of the weak form.
- [x] Artifact pane: a text-extension file that sniffs binary renders a blank pane `bug` `#renderer` `#artifacts` (added 2026-08-12, found by the PR #303 review; **FIXED same day — youcoded PR #308, merge `38a85e95`**: `getViewer` gained a `binaryHint`; registry hits that are text-content viewers (MarkdownView, CodeEditorView, CsvView, and — per the review's finding — HtmlView, whose null-content state was a PERPETUAL "Loading…") route to `BinaryFallback` with its "Open in default app" action. Review verified: UTF-16-BOM files never had a working preview (blank pre-PR → honest fallback now, nothing working taken away), binary files can't reach edit mode, single call site. Known ~1-frame stale-hint window on artifact switch is pre-existing hook architecture, noted for the future.)
- [x] Attachment paperclip opens the file picker biased to images `bug` `#ui` (reported by Destin 2026-08-12, **FIXED same day — youcoded PR #309, merge `f68ad7c3`**)
  The real bias lived in the REMOTE/mobile path: `remote-shim.ts`'s picker set an `accept` whitelist that made phone browsers open a media-biased chooser — removed outright (browsers have no filter dropdown, so all-files is the whole fix there). The desktop native dialog was already All-Files-first since `e83c3ede` (v1.1.0) and gained the requested category options (Images / Markdown & Text / PDFs / Spreadsheets / Documents / Code); Android was already `*/*`. **If the desktop picker still LOOKS images-biased after this, the likely cause is KDE's portal opening in the Recent Files view (dominated by previously-attached screenshots), or an Electron→portal `current_filter` quirk — that would be a new portal-layer investigation, not app code.** Downstream verified: non-image attachments degrade gracefully everywhere (filename chip, path text to CC, path-not-pixels on the native harness — extending native embedding beyond images is a possible follow-up). **Superseded same day by PR #310 (merge `de4ab4e8`): a live D-Bus capture + source-level research proved the desktop bias was Electron's — `GetFilterInfo()` strips the wildcard filter and hardcodes `file_type_index=0`, Chromium appends `*.*` last and omits `current_filter`, so KDE preselects the first concrete filter; Windows skips All-Files-as-default BY DESIGN (electron#43491 + #19492, both closed not-planned). The attachment dialog now passes NO filters on any platform — all files everywhere, no type dropdown. The category-options half of the ask is NOT implementable via Electron's dialog API; routes if ever wanted: upstream Electron patch or an in-app picker. Full diagnosis lives in the handler's WHY comment.**
- [ ] Git surface: `core.quotePath` paths (quotes, backslashes, non-ASCII) read as clean for EVERY entry kind `bug` `#git` (added 2026-08-12, found by the PR #304 review — pre-existing, all entry kinds, not introduced by the conflicted-files fix)
  git C-quotes such paths in porcelain v2 output (verified: `"quo\"te.txt"`, `"\303\274n\303\257code.txt"`); `porcelain.ts` never unquotes, so the `path === rel` match misses and the file reads clean. Fix direction: migrate to `-z` output (already noted as a future migration in `porcelain.ts:79`) or add C-unquoting. A user with an accented filename gets silent no-status today.
- [ ] Git surface: rewrite-staleness residual from the PR #304 review `bug` `#git` (added 2026-08-12 — advisory-grade; the OTHER residual from that review, the unkeyed TerminalRightSlot drawer, was **FIXED same day — youcoded PR #307, merge `6a97e345`**: `key={sessionId}` matching chat view's per-session isolation; review verified remount cost is ~zero net-new IPC and unsaved drafts survive via the module-level draft store, which was designed for exactly this remount)
  After a history REWRITE (amend/rebase) mid-"Show more", stale extraLog entries persist and inflate `--skip` until the review closes — bounded, documented in the WHY comment, but worth a rewrite-detection guard someday.
- [ ] `.claude/rules/sync-spaces.md`'s "sync dots are the ONE sanctioned status-color use" is over-broad — reword or re-scope `bug` `#docs` (added 2026-08-12, found by the PR #304 review)
  Shipped primitives already contradict the global phrasing (Callout warning tone, StatusStrip warn, git footer green/red, the new Conflict badge — and `desktop/CLAUDE.md:120` explicitly sanctions hardcoded status colors incl. amber). The sentence means "sync status specifically must come from sync-dot-state.ts", which is true — scope it to say that. Destin's call on wording since it's a rule file.
- [ ] Decide: should `*.tmp` join sync-spaces `DEFAULT_IGNORES`? `task` `#sync` (added 2026-08-12, surfaced by the PR #296 review — **Destin's call, deliberately NOT slipped into that PR**)
  Temp-then-rename orphans (any `*.tmp` stranded by a crash between write and rename) are not ignored by `sync-spaces/guards.ts:20-41`, so one stranded in a synced dir transports to every device as junk — `transcript-mirror.ts:17-19` documents the hazard and per-writer sweeps mitigate it (PR #296 adds them to cas-write and the two user-tree writers). Adding `*.tmp` to `DEFAULT_IGNORES` would close the class in one line, BUT `isIgnoredPath` also feeds the backup filter, so a user file genuinely named `something.tmp` would silently stop syncing/backing up. Per-writer sweeps make this optional; decide whether the one-line blanket is worth that edge.
- [x] Remote/Android initial history load pulls the FULL transcript — remote-shim `loadHistory` argument order is scrambled `bug` `#remote` `#android` `#perf` (added 2026-08-12, found by the PR #300 review; **FIXED same day — youcoded PR #301, merge `5527fa27`**: shim reordered to match preload with preload-parity defaults so real number/boolean types hit the wire; server now honors a SAFE_ID_RE-gated client `projectSlug` before falling back to the scan; a parameter-ORDER parity test pins the shim against preload (executed against the old code to prove it catches this class). Android impact was worse than filed: the misplaced boolean coerced to slug `"false"`, so Android remote history loaded EMPTY. Review confirmed stale cached-browser shims degrade to exactly the old behavior, no worse. The fix agent's sweep verified loadHistory was the ONLY order mismatch between shim and preload.)
- [x] `tool-views/ToolBody.tsx` has the same unknown-input crash shape ToolCard just fixed `bug` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-12, found by the PR #295 review; **FIXED same day — youcoded PR #306, merge `22108f52`**: all ~30 `input.` accesses in the expanded views hardened via a shared `asString` (extracted to `utils/tool-input.ts`, ToolCard migrated onto it); the AskUserQuestion deep fields got a `normalizeQuestions()` pass so one malformed member degrades instead of crashing — the review traced the normalized-echo through both the native harness path (never read) and the CC hook path (reconstructs exactly the schema's fields; the pre-PR echo was already a reconstruction) and empirically re-confirmed 11/12 tests red-first. The `transcript-watcher.ts:498,743` main-process lie-casts noted in the original entry remain — cosmetic, main-process, no render surface.)
  GrepView (~:694-695, :753) and GlobView (~:765, :773) use `tool.input.path as string | undefined` guarded only by truthiness, so an object `path` crashes `basename()`, and an object `pattern` rendered as a React child throws "Objects are not valid as a React child". ~20 more lie-casts throughout (:242-244, :265-266, :384-386, :510-512, :581-583, :692-695, :764-765, :796-797). Exposure is lower than ToolCard (only on card expand), and a crash is contained by the Chat ErrorBoundary — but it's the identical class; port ToolCard's `asString` idiom. Related main-process instance of the same lie-cast pattern: `transcript-watcher.ts:498,743` (`subagent_type as string || ''`).
- [x] Two remote-server hardening nits from the PR #294 review `bug` `#remote` `#security` (added 2026-08-12, **FIXED same day — youcoded PR #300, merge `7a385879`**: `transcript:read-meta` now mirrors `model:read-last` (non-string → null, resolve inside try — the old throw was an unhandled rejection AND a 30s client hang since no response was ever sent); `session:history` validates the id against `SAFE_ID_RE` (now exported from session-browser as single source of truth) before the fs.access probe; review confirmed no legit id is rejected and the `SAFE_ID_RE.test(undefined)`→"undefined"-string coercion trap is guarded.)
  (a) In the remote `transcript:read-meta` case, `path.resolve(transcriptPath)` at `remote-server.ts:1657` sits *outside* the `try`, and `payload.path || payload` can yield a non-string — a malformed WS frame throws into a floating promise (`ws.on('message', …)` at :685 doesn't catch, and `handleMessage` has no outer try around the switch), so one bad frame can surface as an unhandled rejection instead of an error response. (b) `session:history` (`remote-server.ts:1231`) joins a client-supplied `histSessionId` into a path for an `fs.access` probe BEFORE `loadHistory`'s `SAFE_ID_RE` guard runs — at most a file-existence oracle for `*.jsonl` paths (no content leak), but the probe should validate first. Both small; batch with the next remote-server pass.
- [ ] Pre-existing vitest flake — parallel workers race on the shared temp home `bug` `#testing` (added 2026-08-06)
  Surfaced in three separate tasks during chatsearch phase 1, in a different unrelated file each time (`session-meta-parity.test.ts`, `ipc-handlers.test.ts`), as ENOENT or temp-rename errors. Proven pre-existing by reproducing with all new files stashed. Root cause is parallel vitest workers sharing `/tmp/youcoded-vitest-home` (set in `vitest.config.ts`, wiped once per run by `tests/global-setup.ts`). It passes in isolation and on re-run, so it intermittently fails `verify.sh` and will do the same to unrelated PRs.
- [ ] `remote-server.ts` re-implements the saved-folders store inline — unify it and cover the remote path `bug` `#tech-debt` `#remote` (added 2026-08-06)
  `remote-server.ts` does its own read/parse/find/write of `~/.claude/youcoded-folders.json` in each `folders:*` case instead of importing `saved-folders.ts`, which is the real store (`readFolders`/`writeFolders`, atomic temp-then-rename). As of the per-project-description branch there are **three** copies of that logic: the store module, the `folders:rename` case, and the new `folders:set-description` case. Duplicating rather than refactoring was a deliberate call by Destin 2026-08-06 — the refactor changes the shipped `folders:rename` remote path, and that path has **zero** test coverage (verified: `tests/remote-server.test.ts` contains no `folders` reference; `tests/saved-folders.test.ts` covers only the store module). Doing it inside a description branch was the wrong risk. **Do the test first**: cover `folders:rename` over the WebSocket path, then collapse both cases onto `saved-folders.ts`. Note the inline copies also skip the store's atomic write (plain `writeFile`, not temp-then-rename), so a crash mid-write truncates the user's folder list — that is the concrete bug hiding in the duplication, not just style.
- [x] `validate-plugin-pr.yml` has NEVER fired — wrong branch and wrong paths `bug` `#marketplace` `#ci` (added 2026-08-05, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — wecoded-marketplace PR #67, merge `cbaa74d`.** Branch main→master, paths now top-level with exclusions + plugin.json detection; rebuild loop triple-protected (empty-detection gate, `[skip ci]`, GITHUB_TOKEN pushes don't retrigger); five latent bugs fixed that would have broken the first real run (depth-1 diff, multi-line GITHUB_OUTPUT, false-duplicate on update PRs, always-firing API-key warning, rebuild on non-plugin merges); the also-dead `validate-apple-services*` jobs revived after fixing a jq escape compile error, two SC2209s, and an osascript step that EXECUTED scripts instead of compile-checking them. **Adversarially reviewed twice; second pass clean. What this deliberately did NOT do:** regenerate the drifted index files — the first plugin merge will run `sync.js` for the first time and its bot commit will likely carry a large catch-up diff (Destin's call whether to absorb it in a dedicated PR first). **Residual gaps, non-blocking:** a plugin-DELETION merge skips the rebuild (registry serves the stale entry until the next plugin-touching merge); direct `marketplace.json` edits on master don't regenerate the `.claude-plugin/` mirror; multi-commit direct pushes diff only `HEAD~1..HEAD`.)
  Verified 2026-08-05 while adding `youcoded-chatsearch`: the workflow triggers on `pull_request`/`push` to branch **`main`** filtered to **`plugins/**`**, but `wecoded-marketplace` uses **`master`** and keeps plugins at the **repo top level** (`wecoded-themes-plugin/`, `wecoded-marketplace-publisher/`, `youcoded-chatsearch/`). Both jobs are therefore dead: (1) the PR **validation** job — so the `ALLOWED_TAGS` / `ALLOWED_LIFE_AREAS` / `ALLOWED_AUDIENCE` enums in `scripts/schema.js` are documented as CI-enforced but are not enforced by anything; (2) the merge **rebuild** job — so `index.json` / `skills/index.json` have only ever been regenerated by someone running `node scripts/sync.js` by hand. Consequence seen in practice: the chatsearch branch's regeneration produced a ~20k-line index diff of accumulated upstream drift (sync reported `Added: 154, Deprecated: 20`, of which exactly one addition was the new plugin). Fix = correct the branch and path filters; then decide whether to absorb the drift in one dedicated regeneration PR so feature PRs stay reviewable. `sync.js` also needs `GITHUB_TOKEN` or it hits the anonymous rate limit. **Worse than documented (found 2026-08-12):** the `paths: plugins/**` filter sits on the top-level `on:` block, so it also disables the `validate-apple-services*` jobs later in the same file — their comment claims their trigger "isn't disturbed", but they are dead too.
  **STATUS 2026-07-23 (latest — XWayland attempt executed):** it WORKS. The existing three-window floater runs correctly under XWayland — real positioning, `keepAbove`, clean transparency, sharp at 1.5× on KDE, visually confirmed by Destin. Two flags are required: a **real argv** `electron . --ozone-platform=x11` (`app.commandLine.appendSwitch('ozone-platform',…)` and `ELECTRON_OZONE_PLATFORM_HINT` are both **silently ineffective** on Electron 41 — verified) and `--use-angle=vulkan` to stop a GPU-process SIGSEGV at `EGL_CreateWindowSurface` (default ANGLE GL crashed 3×/launch; vulkan 0). Also found + fixed a real Electron bug: at 1.5× fractional scale `setPosition()` inflates frameless windows every call, ballooning the chat to 1851×1526 and drifting the group apart — fixed by re-asserting fixed size via `setBounds` (`min/max size does NOT clamp it`). **Shelved as EXPERIMENTAL, not shipped** — draft PR itsdestin/youcoded#239, branch `fix/linux-xwayland-floater`. Why: the ozone backend is a whole-process launch-time choice (can't run the main app native-Wayland and only the buddy on XWayland), production enablement is unimplemented (needs self re-exec or launcher flag), XWayland sharpness is compositor-specific (blurry on GNOME), mixed-DPI multi-monitor breaks, and the GPU flag is untested off this hardware. **Decision: prefer finding a native-Wayland path; if XWayland is ever adopted, hide it behind a user toggle rather than forcing it on all Linux users.** Evidence: `docs/active/investigations/2026-07-23-buddy-overlay-wayland-presentation.md`.
  **STATUS 2026-07-23 (earlier):** the one-window overlay rewrite was BUILT and merged DORMANT (youcoded PR #214 — BuddyManager seam + overlay behind `YOUCODED_BUDDY_STRATEGY=overlay` + 7 live-found fixes; zero behavior change on all platforms). It cannot be enabled: `setIgnoreMouseEvents` is a probe-verified TOTAL no-op on native Wayland (with and without `forward`), so the fullscreen overlay eats every click — the design's load-bearing primitive doesn't exist. The 2026-07-17 rejection of XWayland below was **falsified** by the workbench (FINDINGS Round 3/5c): KDE's default `XwaylandClientsScale` renders XWayland Electron SHARP at 1.5× fractional scale, and the smear fix shipped in the Electron 41.10.3 bump. Under XWayland the EXISTING three-window floater works (setPosition, always-on-top, transparency all pass) — remaining blocker is a GPU crash in the full app (ANGLE null-deref at EGL_CreateWindowSurface; minimal probes survive) with an untested workaround list (`--use-angle=gl`, `--disable-gpu-sandbox`, `--in-process-gpu`; worktree `worktrees/xwayland-floater` exists). Full evidence + next steps: `docs/active/investigations/2026-07-23-buddy-overlay-wayland-presentation.md`. Original analysis kept below for context.
  On native Wayland (Electron ≥38.2 default), the buddy floater is unusable: it can't be dragged (stuck centered), and edge-anchored peek/sink/lean animations misfire inside the fixed 112×112 window. Root cause is architectural, not a code bug — Wayland forbids apps from programmatically positioning (`setPosition` is a no-op, `getPosition` returns `[0,0]`) or introspecting global window coordinates, and the floater's entire drag model is renderer-pointermove → `buddy:move-mascot` IPC → main-process `setPosition` every frame across THREE separate transparent frameless BrowserWindows (mascot + chat + bar, see `buddy-window-manager.ts`, `buddy-bar-geometry.ts`, `BuddyMascot.tsx`). Confirmed against Electron docs + issues #52204/#48833/#40886; repro env: CachyOS, KDE Plasma (KWin 6.7.3), Wayland, single 1.5×-scaled panel. The proper fix (per Wayland devs + Electron maintainers) is to collapse the three windows into ONE fullscreen transparent always-on-top window and position the mascot/chat/bar as absolutely-positioned DOM inside it — dragging becomes a local CSS write (no IPC, no platform permission, dodges the transparency-smear bug class too). Hard parts priced in: rewiring the 7 cross-webContents IPC pushes + session-subscription-by-webContents.id into in-app React state, and hand-managing dynamic click-through regions (`setIgnoreMouseEvents(forward:true)` + hover-region tracking) across the dead transparent area — the cursor-timing-sensitive debugging the current per-window design gets for free. Do NOT do the `--ozone-platform=x11` XWayland stopgap as the fix (considered 2026-07-17, rejected): it blurs the whole app at 1.5× fractional scaling AND still needs an Electron bump for the separate transparent-window smear bug (electron#50541, propagating to 41-x). This rewrite SUBSUMES the "scene-companion follow physics — window padding redesign" item under Features (the padding/click-through problem disappears in a one-window model). Prototype the click-through + hover behavior in a workbench first, like the rig/peek work.

## Bugs

- [ ] **Android terminal mode hides the wallpaper to reveal a native terminal that no longer exists** `bug` `#themes` `#android` (added 2026-08-12, found while investigating desktop resize repaint)
  `globals.css`'s `html[data-platform="android"][data-view-mode="terminal"] #theme-bg, … #theme-pattern { display: none }` rule exists, per its own comment, "so the native TerminalView (rendered below the transparent WebView in ChatScreen.kt) shows through". That TerminalView is **gone** — `ChatScreen.kt:12-15` documents removing the TerminalView Compose block, `applyTerminalColors`, and the BaseTerminalViewClient/TerminalSession imports, and `youcoded/CLAUDE.md` states xterm.js in the WebView has been the sole Android terminal renderer since Tier 2.
  So on Android in terminal mode the wallpaper and pattern layers are hidden to reveal… whatever Compose paints behind the transparent WebView, which is a hardcoded `Color(0xFF111111)` (`ChatScreen.kt:30`, with `WebViewHost.kt:44` setting `Color.TRANSPARENT` on the WebView). Under a light theme that reads as a dark rectangle where the wallpaper should be. After `fix/resize-paint-race` gives `html` an opaque `background-color: var(--canvas)`, the exposed area becomes the theme canvas instead of `#111111` — better, but the rule is still hiding the wallpaper for no remaining reason.
  Fix is probably to **split the selector, not delete the rule**: the `body[data-mode="buddy-mascot"] #theme-bg, body[data-mode="buddy-chat"] #theme-bg` half of the same rule is still correct and load-bearing for buddy transparency. Wants an `assembleDebug` eyeball on a light theme in terminal mode to confirm the symptom before changing anything.

- [ ] **Slug-repair follow-ups from the 2026-08-12 final branch review** `bug` `#slugs` `#tech-debt` (added 2026-08-12; deferred by the final whole-branch review of `fix/project-slug-encoding` — all safe-direction, none block the merge)
  Four items the review triaged DEFER, recorded here so they survive the session: (1) **fork snapshots re-accumulate every launch** — an unresolved case-C fork re-snapshots both copies (~10MB for the `26d919ff` pair) into a fresh timestamped quarantine dir each startup until the fork is manually resolved; fast-follow is a `surfacedForks` set in `~/.youcoded/slug-repair-state.json` gating re-snapshot (~10 lines). (2) **Stale deferred-key sweep** — `state.deferred` entries are only cleared when the session re-appears in findings; a session whose mis-filed location vanishes without a resolving finding leaves a dead key forever (harmless growth). (3) **Windows lowercase-drive over-cap probe** (spec risk 6) — no real-CC fixture pins hash-of-drive-normalized vs hash-of-raw on a >200-char lowercase-drive path; needs a probe run on a Windows machine. (4) **Kotlin non-BMP divergence** — Kotlin regex matches per code point, JS per UTF-16 unit, so an emoji in a path would slug differently on Android vs CC; unreachable today (only ASCII `canonicalHome()` is passed), WHY-commented in `CcProjectSlug.kt`; add an emoji pair next probe run.

- [ ] **Settings → Backup & Sync takes the whole workbench down with "YouCoded failed to start"** `bug` `#workbench` `#sync` `#ui` (added 2026-08-12, found while building the permissions screen in the workbench)
  Opening Settings → Backup & Sync in `bash scripts/run-workbench.sh` throws `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')` and the RootErrorBoundary replaces the app with the full "YouCoded failed to start" screen. **Reproduced against a clean master workbench during the M5 2a session — pre-existing, NOT caused by that branch.**
  **Root cause, read out of source rather than inferred from the symptom:** `mock-shim.ts` has no `sync` namespace at all (zero hits for `getStatus` or a `sync` impl), so `window.claude.sync.getStatus()` falls through to `withCatchAll`'s `apply` trap, which returns `delay([])` — an empty ARRAY. `[]` is truthy, so `SyncPanel.tsx:1479`'s `status && status.syncedCategories.length` reads `.length` off `undefined`. Note that line is the outlier on its own screen: every neighbouring status read (`:362`, `:384`, `:387`) is defensively `?.`-guarded, so the product-side hardening is a one-character fix and the real work is a `sync` impl in the shim.
  **`workbench-boot-check.mjs` cannot catch this class and says so** — its header documents that it "only exercises MOUNT", and its `ROUTES` list covers frames and scenarios, never a Settings sub-panel. Anything reachable only by clicking into a panel is invisible to it, which is why a green boot check accompanied this crash.

- [ ] **`NativeSessionHost.askPermission` has zero callers** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#permissions` (added 2026-08-12, found while wiring M5 2a's revocation path)
  `rg "askPermission"` over the whole youcoded repo returns exactly one line — the declaration itself (`native-session-host.ts:243` on `feat/native-permissions-ui`, `:230` on master). Its doc comment says "Task 12's `decide()` will call this"; what actually shipped hands each `HarnessSession` an `askUser: (req) => this.broker.ask(req)` closure (`:415`), which reaches the same broker without ever going through this method. Dead code, and knip will not flag it because it is an exported class method — the same shape as `HookRelay.closeSocket()` above. Either delete it or record why the API surface is kept.

- [ ] **`review-harness.mjs`'s API-key scrub does not actually stop the model reading the key** `bug` `#harness-eval` `#security` (added 2026-08-12)
  `test-engine/review-harness.mjs:113` reads `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` into a local and then `delete process.env.OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, with a comment stating this stops the key reaching a saved transcript. It does not. `delete` compiles to `unsetenv`, which edits the **in-heap** environ array; it never rewrites the `mm->env_start..env_end` region the kernel exposes at `/proc/<pid>/environ`. Every descendant of the runner can still read the parent's original environment — and the Bash tool the model drives is exactly such a descendant.
  **Measured 2026-08-12**, reproducing the runner's exact shape (key arrives in env at exec, captured, deleted, then a child spawned with `env: process.env`): the child reports `inherited-env: 0` — the delete does work for the child's own copy — but `parent-environ: 1` from `/proc/$PPID/environ`. `ps eww -p $PPID` leaks it too. The commonly-checked channels (`env`, `printenv`, `ps -eo args`, `/proc/self/cmdline`) all read clean, which is why this survived: the obvious probes all pass.
  **Why it is reachable, not theoretical:** `BATTERY_PROMPT` area 5 instructs the model to "test env var persistence across calls", so it is already poking at the environment; anything it reads lands in `run.events`, which the CLI writes verbatim to `docs/active/investigations/harness-review-runs/<date>/<model>.json`. Those transcripts are gitignored, so the realistic blast radius is local disk plus whatever the model chose to quote back in its review — not a repo leak.
  **Severity is moderate, not urgent:** a dev-only script, an OpenRouter-scoped key, run by hand against models that are semi-trusted by construction. But the key has been rotated for unrelated reasons before, and the rule file repeated the same false assurance ("the CLI deletes it from `process.env` before any run"), so the claim needed correcting wherever it appears. **Corrected 2026-08-13** in `.claude/rules/harness-evaluator.md` (renamed from `harness-review-runner.md`).
  **Fix, implemented on `feat/harness-evaluator`:** the new CLI passes the credential over the child's **stdin** rather than argv or environment — nothing in `/proc` for a descendant to read — reads it from `--key-file`, and **refuses to start** if `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is in its own environment at all. It also allowlists the child environment, so `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` and anything else in the operator's shell stop riding along into every tool subprocess. Guard: `tests/harness-eval-key-leak.test.ts`, with a negative control that must report LEAKED.
  **Still open:** `review-harness.mjs` itself is untouched and still leaks. It is now otherwise superseded — it imports its runner logic from `dist/main/harness/eval/`, and the evaluator's `harness-battery` case runs the same battery. **Decide: retire it, or port the three fixes.** Retiring also drops its one unique behaviour, appending each model's free-form review into `docs/active/investigations/`.

- [ ] **A trimmed-for-the-wire image is still claimed as "already visible" by the dedupe cache** `bug` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-08-11)
  `fitToContext` drops oldest messages from the outgoing request only — `this.history` is untouched — so an image can scroll out of what the model actually sees while `shownImages` still vouches for it. The model then gets `[image not re-attached: <path> is unchanged and already visible earlier in this conversation]` about a picture it can no longer see, and no image, until that file's mtime changes. Same misleading-text-plus-silent-non-delivery class the rest of youcoded#293 closed; this is the one path left open, and it is documented at the `shownImages` field rather than papered over.
  **Bounded:** needs the prune-protected window to exceed the fit budget (context under ~8,500 tokens) AND `supportsVision: true`, which for a local engine requires an explicit registry entry — i.e. a registry-declared small local vision model. Everything larger is covered because compaction re-estimates un-trimmed history at each turn start and fires first.
  **Do not "fix" it by clearing the cache at `fitToContext`** — it runs on every request, so an unconditional clear defeats dedupe entirely and a count-diff latches permanently true once history outgrows the window. Two reviewers rejected both. Intended fix: re-key `shownImages` to `Map<path, {mtime, toolCallId}>` and reconcile against the fitted window after trimming — exact, no over-clearing, ~8 lines.

- [ ] OpenRouter session start reads the model catalog twice `bug` `#native-runtime` `#perf` (added 2026-08-11)
  `contextLengthFor` and `visionSupportFor` each independently call `ModelCatalog.get()`, and `get()`/`ensureFresh()` re-read the on-disk cache file with no in-memory memoization. On a stale cache that is a duplicated 15s-bounded fetch pair rather than a shared one; on the happy path it is a second `readFileSync` + full `JSON.parse` + re-parse of the whole OpenRouter payload, synchronously on the main process, per create/resume/swap. Non-OpenRouter bindings short-circuit and are unaffected. Disclosed honestly in both closures' comments. Fix is memoization inside `ModelCatalog`, not another short-circuit.

- [x] Chat/terminal toggle's sliding indicator disappears after a narrow→wide round trip `bug` `#renderer` `#ui` (added 2026-08-11; FIXED same day — youcoded `a39e287d`. Investigation: `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-11-chat-terminal-toggle-indicator-disappears.md`)
  Root cause was ownership, not math. Endpoint geometry lived in four `--pill-*` custom properties written imperatively onto the container node, while the `measured` readiness flag was `HeaderBar` state that was only ever set to `true`. Crossing 640px swaps `<NarrowViewToggle/>` for a different element type, so React unmounted the node holding the properties — and the new one rendered with `measured` still `true`, so `left`/`width` fell back to `auto` and an empty absolutely-positioned div collapsed to zero size. Intermittent only because any of the four re-measure triggers silently repaired it.
  The same ownership gap explained the separate theme-switch jank: the trigger list (mount, `showToggleLabels`, `document.fonts.ready`, `window.resize`) could not cover theme swaps, since `fonts.ready` describes *current* pending font work rather than subscribing to later stylesheets, and a theme swap is not specified to emit `resize`. Fix replaces the enumerated triggers with observed geometry: `WideViewToggle` owns endpoints in state tied to its own mount and measures an inert sizing layer holding two non-animating endpoint copies, so any event that actually changes layout changes the observed boxes. Live-following the animated buttons was tried three times before (`ae5776ee`, `68462e9b`, `a0103014`) and is why the sizing layer exists — the WHY comment at the observer carries those SHAs. Environmental corrections snap via `data-geometry-syncing`; only `viewMode` changes animate. Guards: `WideViewToggle.test.tsx` (19 tests incl. the narrow→wide remount regression), `tests/toggle-motion-policy.test.ts`.

- [ ] **Presence can wedge OFF permanently — `suspended` is clearable only by a `powerMonitor` event** `bug` `#social` `#desktop` (added 2026-08-11; **spec: `docs/active/specs/2026-08-11-presence-self-healing-design.md`**)
  Found from a friend row reading `Last seen 7/26/2026` on 2026-08-11 while the person was still using the app, signed in, not incognito, on a MacBook. **No data or server defect** — `users.last_seen_at` is accurate; the client simply never opened a socket again. Ruled out by inspection: client caching (`GameLobby.tsx:238` refetches on mount, no cache), duplicate accounts (`identities` is keyed `(provider, provider_user_id)`), server version-gating of old clients (none exists in `worker/src/social/routes.ts`), session expiry (`SESSION_MAX_IDLE_SEC` is 90 days), and column-type drift (`last_seen_at` is INTEGER with a `MAX()` no-go-backwards guard).
  **The defect:** `presence-socket.ts:114` composes `rendererDesired && !suspended && !idle`, and `social-handlers.ts:126-127` is the *entire* lifecycle of `suspended` — set on `powerMonitor` `suspend`, cleared only on `resume`. If that clearing edge is ever missed, presence is off for the rest of the process lifetime: user input can't clear it (the 15s poller only writes the `idle` axis), sign-out/sign-in can't (that writes `rendererDesired`), and the reconnect engine can't (it isn't retrying). Only a full quit-and-relaunch does. macOS is the worst case *by construction* — the comment at `social-handlers.ts:120` deliberately relies on dark wakes not firing `resume`, but nothing compensates for a missed **real** wake, and a MacBook runs for weeks of lid-close cycles without being quit. The suspend handler's last act before wedging is a clean close, which stamps `last_seen_at` — so the frozen date is the last lid-close with a working socket.
  **Diagnostic property worth keeping:** every socket teardown also stamps the timestamp (`presence-room.ts` lines 202/237/243), so any fault that connects-then-drops yields a *moving* date. A frozen date means nothing has connected since. That is what rules out the idle gate, OS suspend, Wi-Fi drops, Android Doze, and the old-Android missing-ping flapping as candidates — all of them would keep the date advancing.
  Second wedge, same machinery: presence uses `noToken: 'wait'`, and `reconnecting-ws.ts:81-86` schedules **no retry** when the token is missing at connect time (only `'poll'` does); `usePresence` re-invokes only on `signedIn`/`incognito`/`isLeader` changes.
  Trap for whoever implements the obvious fix: a timer that re-asserts desire does NOT work — `setDesired(true)` on a desired-but-disconnected engine cancels the pending backoff timer and reconnects immediately (`reconnecting-ws.ts:124-139`, deliberate), so it would defeat capped backoff during an outage and spam `onReplay` on healthy sockets. The spec keys the repair on an explicit stall predicate instead. Safety argument for the whole fix: `idle` is the correctness gate and `suspended` is only a fast-offline optimization, so over-clearing `suspended` is bounded by `idle` and cannot resurrect the 2026-07-22 ghost-socket bug.
  **Not verifiable here** — the workspace's only machine is Linux, so the macOS `resume` behavior can't be reproduced; the fix is designed not to depend on knowing it. Field test on the affected machine: a full quit-and-relaunch restoring Online is positive evidence, since a relaunch is the only thing that clears the latch today.

- [ ] **Any 401 silently destroys the local account session** — no notice, no log `bug` `#social` `#desktop` (added 2026-08-11)
  `handler-utils.ts` wraps every social and marketplace handler with `if (!result.ok && result.status === 401) store.signOut()`. One 401 from any of them unhooks the user's account with zero feedback; `usePresence` then drops presence, so the symptom is indistinguishable from the wedge above (frozen `last_seen_at`, friends see them offline forever) and the user only finds out by opening the friends panel. Deleting the sign-out would be worse — the UI would claim signed-in while every call failed — so the fix is to make it *announced*, not to remove it. Scoped separately from the presence repair (auth surface, own blast radius); see Part 4 of `docs/active/specs/2026-08-11-presence-self-healing-design.md`.

- [x] **master was RED on Windows and macOS CI** — four Bash-cwd / Grep-path tests `bug` `#native-runtime` `#ci` `#harness` (added 2026-08-11; **FIXED 2026-08-11 — youcoded PR #291, merge `71c4014a`**. Spec + plan archived: `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-11-harness-cross-platform-path-vocabulary.md`)
  Broke at `eba51705` ("Merge harness tool honesty + review runner", 2026-08-10) and still failing at master tip. Ubuntu passes; the `build` aggregate passes. Failing tests, verified identical on master and on every branch cut from it — **four distinct cases across six platform/case combinations**, not six tests:
  - **Windows:** `Bash reports the tracked cwd after a cd, so the model never has to guess` · `ANSI stripping does not disturb the cwd sentinel` · `Grep returns workspace-relative paths for targets inside the workspace`
  - **macOS:** `Bash always states the cwd and exit code` · `Bash reports the tracked cwd after a cd…` · `ANSI stripping does not disturb the cwd sentinel`
  The first three are in `tests/harness-tools-core.test.ts` (`:468`, `:491`, `:521`); the Grep case is in `tests/harness-tool-bounds.test.ts:53`.
  **CORRECTED 2026-08-11 — the original `__YC_CWD__` sentinel hypothesis below was WRONG.** The sentinel works correctly on all three platforms. These are **two independent defects**, both real product bugs, neither a test defect: (1) `bash.ts:499` compares the shell's reported cwd to `startCwd` with `path.resolve`, which neither follows symlinks nor expands Windows 8.3 short names — so two spellings of ONE directory read as a change and the canonical form lands in `setShellCwd` and the `[cwd: …]` metadata line (macOS `/var`→`/private/var`; Windows `RUNNER~1`→`runneradmin`). Reproduced on **Linux** with a symlinked root, so this is not a CI-only artifact — it hits any symlinked workspace root on any platform. (2) `glob.ts:232` normalizes separators to `/`; `grep.ts` prints ripgrep's stdout verbatim and rg uses the platform separator, so Windows gets `src\a.ts` from Grep and `src/a.ts` from Glob — the sibling Glob test passing on Windows is the proof Grep is the outlier. Fix, decisions, rejected alternatives, and the traps (rebasing must not outrun the scope guard; `--path-separator=/` alone silently breaks the currently-agreeing external-directory case; `guards.ts:25 canonicalize()` must NOT be reused for output because it lowercases on Windows) are all in the spec.
  **Why this matters beyond the red X:** the desktop CI is the ONLY thing exercising these two platforms — `scripts/verify.sh` runs on the developer's machine, which is Linux, so a local green says nothing about them. Two PRs (#289, #290) were merged on 2026-08-11 with these failures present, after confirming the same failures on master first; they are inherited, not caused. But the longer master stays red the less any future red means, which is the same "trains sessions to ignore the unignorable check" failure already recorded for the `web-fetch.ts` ast-grep entry.

- [x] **Sync health warnings never clear themselves — "No internet" sat under "All synced · 1m ago"** `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-08-11, reported by Destin from his live app, **SHIPPED 2026-08-11 — youcoded PR #292, merge `883bf3ad`**)
  `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` ran **exactly once per app run**, at the end of `start()`. It owns three codes — `OFFLINE`, `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `PERSONAL_STALE` — and all three describe conditions that change *while the app is open*, but nothing re-evaluated them. A launch that lost the race with the WiFi coming up (or any resolver hiccup — the probe is a single `dns.lookup('github.com')` with a 5s timeout) wrote `OFFLINE` into `.sync-warnings.json`, and it stayed there for the rest of the session. Destin's screenshot is the two states side by side: a red "No internet — can't reach the network" card directly under a green "All synced · GitHub · itsdestin · instant sync on · 1m ago" header, four devices listed, self "Synced just now".
  This is the residue the **PR #254 entry below predicted in its own last sentence** — *"Landing #248 alone does NOT close this: any transient warning (a real OFFLINE at launch) still pins the row red until restart."* #254 fixed the renderer half (the Settings row read a frozen mount-time snapshot instead of the live file); after it, every surface reads the file faithfully — and the **file** was the stale thing. Worth noting as a pattern: three separate entries here (#248, #254, this one) are the same bogus-warning symptom at three different layers, each fix correct and each insufficient alone.
  Fix: re-run the check every 60s, cleared in `stop()`; the periodic run passes `probeBackends: false` so `autoDetectBackend`'s rclone shell-out (35s, measured on the Z13) stays a launch-only cost; `OFFLINE` now needs two consecutive failed probes so a launch race can't produce it at all; a dismissed code stays dismissed for the app run (else the re-check resurrects it a minute later); the file is rewritten only when the set actually changes, with `createdEpoch` excluded from that comparison — which also upgrades the field's meaning to "when this condition was first seen". Guard: `tests/sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts`, 9 cases, all red on the parent commit; the load-bearing one is "start() keeps re-checking, and stop() ends it", since the sweep logic itself was never the missing piece.

- [ ] **Pasting a bare filepath was eaten as a slash command** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-10, Destin during the M1–M3 dogfood) — **PARTIALLY FIXED, merged 2026-08-10 (youcoded `d8c4d59c` on PR #287). STAYS OPEN: the shipped gate does not close the class.** The review of #287 measured the residue against the real dispatcher — `/My Files/notes.md` → `invoke-skill skill="my" args="Files/notes.md"`, `/Users Shared/x/y.md` → `skill="users"`, `/README.md` → `skill="readme.md"`, and bare `/etc` / `/home` — all still consumed, input still deleted. The gate tests the COMMAND WORD for a second `/`, so any path whose first segment lacks one sails through.
  **DEPRIORITIZED 2026-08-10 — Destin is not concerned about the residual cases.** The common shape (`/home/destin/notes.md`) is fixed and shipped; what remains are root-level files and paths with a space in the first segment. Do not surface this as pending work or spend a session on it unless he raises it. The analysis below is kept because it is measured and would otherwise have to be redone.
  **It is not a discriminator problem, so tightening the string test cannot fix it.** `InputBar.tsx` does `void runNativeSlashAction(...); return true;` — fire-and-forget — and `sendMessage` is synchronous, so the caller clears the draft before the skill result is known. `runNativeSkill` DOES return `false` on not-found and main already knows the skill does not exist; the renderer discards that answer. Three shapes, needing a product decision: (a) restore the draft on failure — smallest, but the box flickers and a bogus "Couldn't run /my" toast still fires for text that was never a command; (b) fall through to sending it as a normal message when the skill is not found — matches what CC sessions already do, best outcome, but a genuine typo like `/compcat` would go to the model instead of toasting; (c) resolve against the skill catalog in the dispatcher — closes the class at the source but reverses the standing decision recorded at `slash-command-dispatcher.ts:76-85`.
  Pasting an absolute path into the input box to ask about a file toasted a "skill not found" warning and DELETED the input. `/home/destin/notes.md` parsed as the command word `/home`, missed every case in the dispatcher's switch, and fell to the `/skill-name` branch (M3 item 1), which returns an `invoke-skill` intent; `routeSlashResult` consults `nativeAction` BEFORE `handled` by design, so the native route claimed the input as consumed and the caller cleared the box. Claude Code sessions never showed it — they reach `passthrough` and send the path as ordinary text, which is what the fix restores for native. The gate tests the command word only, so a path passed as an argument still works. Guard: `tests/slash-command-filepath.test.ts`.
  **Worth noting for whoever touches the dispatcher next:** this is the second bug caused by `/skill-name` riding the `handled:false` branch. That branch means "not a known command", and it now carries an intent, so ANY unrecognized `/`-prefixed text becomes a skill invocation. Two shapes are known to reach it wrongly (a filepath; anything with a second slash). A URL pasted with a leading slash, or a regex, would too. The narrow fix is in; the general question — whether an unrecognized token should be *assumed* to be a skill — is not settled.

- [x] **A permission prompt can describe a DIFFERENT tool than the one it approves** — *raises the priority of M5 item 3 (no way to revoke an "Always allow"); see program §6* `bug` `#native-runtime` `#chat-reducer` `#permissions` (added 2026-08-09, Destin during the M1–M3 dogfood; **merged 2026-08-10, PR #287**)
  Reported sequence, native session: `Bash 1 → "Allow Bash 1?" → Bash 1 active → Read 1 → "Allow Bash 1?" → Read 1 active`. The card names and describes the earlier tool; approving it runs the new one. **This is a consent bug, not a display bug** — the user is shown one action and authorizes another, on the app's highest-stakes control. Treat it as higher severity than its "sometimes" framing suggests.
  **Mechanism, confirmed present on master (not reproduced, but the code path is unambiguous).** `PERMISSION_REQUEST` binds an incoming ask to an existing card through a three-tier match (`chat-reducer.ts:1135-1162`): (1) same name AND identical input, (2) first running tool with the same name, (3) **`anyRunningId` — the first running tool of ANY name.** Tier 3 is the bug: it hands the ask to an unrelated card, and the code comment at `:1177-1179` records that swapping the input in was *deliberately declined* there ("would render tool Y's name over tool X's input, a worse lie"). So tier 3 knowingly leaves the earlier tool's name AND input on a card whose buttons now authorize a different tool. Native asks reach this same reducer path — they ride the CC `hook:event` channel by design (`ipc-handlers.ts:2372`) into `hook-dispatcher.ts:25` — so this is NOT native-specific, though native is where it was seen.
  **Two conditions must coincide, which is why it is intermittent:** a tool of a different name is still `status: 'running'`, AND the new tool's own card does not exist yet, so tiers 1–2 miss. **The first condition is very likely the stuck-"running" bug logged directly below** — an orphaned card poisons this matcher for the rest of the session. Fixing that one may reduce this one's frequency without touching the real hole; do not let that mask it.
  **Answering "did we already fix this?": partly, and not this part.** `a4232a18` (merged 2026-08-09, `fix/askuserquestion-stale-card`) fixed the adjacent tier-2 case — a name-matched card showing an earlier call's input, which is how the second AskUserQuestion of a session re-displayed the first one's options. Tier 3 was consciously excluded from that fix. Destin's installed build predates it regardless.
  **FIXED — merged 2026-08-10 on PR #287 (youcoded `fad49656`).** The gating question is answered: `git show d6c90c1a^` shows tier 3 WAS the original naive implementation (its own comment: "the arbitrary first-running-tool fallback"); tiers 1–2 were added in front of it and it was never removed. Vestigial, not load-bearing — deleted. An unmatched ask now falls through to the pre-existing synthetic card, which describes the ask's own payload. Guard: `chat-reducer.test.ts` → "PERMISSION_REQUEST tool identity".
  **Original fix direction, kept for the record:** an ask should never bind to a card whose `toolName` differs from the ask's. Dropping tier 3 lets it fall through to the synthetic-card path that already exists at `:1203`, which mints a card from the ask's own payload and is reclaimed by `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_USE`'s merge when the real event lands — honest by construction. **Before removing it, establish why tier 3 was added** (tier 2 already degrades on input mismatch, so tier 3 only earns its place if some producer reports a name the transcript does not — MCP's `mcp__server__tool` namespacing is the obvious suspect). If such a case exists, reconcile the names rather than keeping a blind fallback.

- [ ] **A resumed session shows tool calls still "running" — a tool cannot be live in a session that was closed** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#chat-reducer` (added 2026-08-09, Destin during the M1–M3 dogfood)
  Reported symptom: after resuming a session, some tool cards still render as active/running. As Destin put it, the tool obviously can't be running — the session was closed and reopened, so whatever it was doing died with the process.
  **Hypothesis, NOT yet reproduced or confirmed** — read the code before trusting it. Replay dispatches the same `TRANSCRIPT_*` actions the live path uses: `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_USE` creates the card at `status: 'running'` (`chat-reducer.ts:243`) and adds it to `activeTurnToolIds`; `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_RESULT` closes it. The only thing that reaps a tool left running is `endTurn()` (`chat-reducer.ts:171-181`), which flips every still-`running`/`awaiting-approval` id in `activeTurnToolIds` to `'failed'` — and it only fires on a turn-ending action. So a transcript whose last turn was cut off mid-tool-call (app quit, crash, kill) contains a `tool_use` with no matching result AND no turn-complete after it, and replaying it reproduces the orphan faithfully. Nothing appears to run at the *end* of replay to reap what the transcript never closed. `HISTORY_LOADED` is not the culprit — it only rebuilds text segments and never reconstructs tool calls at all.
  **Check both providers before scoping.** The suspected mechanism is reducer-level and provider-agnostic (CC replays via `transcript-watcher.ts:469 getHistory`, native via its own eager transcript load on resume), so this may well affect Claude Code sessions identically — do not assume it is native-only just because it was found in a native session.
  **FIXED FOR NATIVE SESSIONS ONLY — merged 2026-08-10 on PR #287 (`fad49656`, plus `c7cf8692` for the buddy window).** The replay handler now appends a synthesized `replay-complete` marker and the reducer reaps orphans through the existing `endTurn()`.
  **Second surface, found reviewing #287 and fixed in the same PR:** the original fix landed in `App.tsx` only. `BubbleFeed` feeds its OWN `chatReducer` instance — the buddy is a separate `BrowserWindow` and cannot share the main window's `ChatProvider` — and already called `requestTranscriptReplay`, so main was delivering the marker to that window all along; it just had no `case` for it, and the card kept spinning in the buddy feed. Guard: `tests/transcript-event-surface-parity.test.ts`, which measures the App/BubbleFeed divergence rather than asserting a single case. **That test also records three further gaps in its `KNOWN_GAPS` ledger — `user-interrupt`, `skill-invoked`, `context-clear` — none investigated.** `user-interrupt` is the likeliest real bug of this same family: ESC in the main window ends the turn, and the buddy would not see it. **Claude Code sessions still have this bug, deliberately:** the reap is gated on a `sessionIdle` flag because the SAME replay fires when a window re-docks a genuinely mid-turn session, where the running tool is real and must not be failed. Only `NativeSessionHost.isIdle()` (`entry.inFlight`) can affirm idleness; CC has no equivalent signal, so it reports false and keeps today's behaviour rather than risk failing a live tool. **Closing the CC half needs a mid-turn signal for PTY sessions that does not exist today** — that is the remaining work on this entry, and it is why this stays [ ] after the branch merges. Guard: `chat-reducer.test.ts` → "TRANSCRIPT_REPLAY_COMPLETE" (incl. a test that a non-idle replay does NOT reap).
  **Do not render these as "complete."** We do not know whether the tool finished before the process died, and a card claiming success for work that may never have happened is the failure mode `docs/error-message-standards.md` exists to prevent. The honest terminal state is failed/interrupted with a reason that names the real cause — the session was closed while this was running — which is exactly what `endTurn`'s existing `error` field already carries. The likely fix is to reap orphans once replay completes, reusing `endTurn`'s treatment rather than adding a second notion of "tool that never finished."

- [ ] **Community themes can inject arbitrary `@keyframes` — the sanitizer never looks at `animation`** `bug` `#performance` `#themes` `#marketplace` (added 2026-08-07)
  `sanitizeCSS` (`theme-validator.ts:152-174`) strips exactly five constructs — `@import`, external-protocol `url()`, `expression()`, `javascript:`, `-moz-binding` — and never inspects `animation`, `animation-iteration-count`, or `@keyframes`. The two lint passes (`lintCustomCss`, `lintCustomCssSelectors`) are `console.warn` only and check z-index and selector names. Meanwhile the blessed `KNOWN_THEME_HOOKS` targets (`.assistant-bubble`, `.header-bar`, `.input-bar-container`) are permanently-mounted chrome, and because the `[data-reduced-effects]` / `prefers-reduced-motion` rules are selector-specific, **Reduced Effects would not disable a theme's animation either**. So an installed theme can impose an uncapped ~30%-of-a-core perpetual cost on every user, with no switch that turns it off. No shipped theme does this today (`golden-sunbreak`, `meadow-mist`, `halftone-dimension` custom_css contain no `animation`) — this is a ceiling problem, not a live one. Second vector: `sanitize-rig-svg.ts:13` strips SMIL but keeps `class`/`style`, so a theme SVG can attach app-owned `comp-*` keyframes to arbitrarily many elements. Fix is probably a cap (force `steps()`, or reject `infinite`) rather than a ban, since animation is a legitimate theming tool.

- [ ] **The animation frame-budget cost ships to Android and remote browsers too, and is unmeasured there** `bug` `#performance` `#android` `#remote` (added 2026-08-07)
  Verified reach, not assumed: `scripts/build-web-ui.sh:19-26` copies `dist/renderer/*` verbatim into `app/src/main/assets/web`, the `bundleWebUi` Gradle task runs before every APK build, and CI does the same — so the identical `globals.css` (with `buddy.css` + `mascot.css` folded in) ships in the APK. `rg 'setFrameRate|preferredDisplayModeId|refreshRate'` across `app/src/main/kotlin` returns **zero hits**, so nothing caps frame rate; `WebViewHost.kt:61` sets `LAYER_TYPE_HARDWARE` and the WebView presents at the device's active refresh rate. Phones are commonly 120 Hz, always on battery, and Reduced Effects defaults off — with no fan noise to prompt a user to go find the toggle. Remote is the same story via `remote-server.ts:251` (serves the same `dist/renderer`), except the **client's** display governs, so a remote user on a 240 Hz monitor pays 240 fps while the host desktop is still presenting simultaneously. Mitigating: mascot rigs and companions are excluded from both surfaces (`Icons.tsx:281`), and Android's `PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK` means a slept screen stops presenting. **Unmeasured:** the per-frame cost coefficient on mobile Chromium/ARM — the 29-33% figure is one Linux/amdgpu/180Hz machine. Measure before scoping any fix.

- [ ] **Unverified claim: "the marketplace grid pre-blurs ONE backdrop element"** `bug` `#docs` `#performance` (added 2026-08-07)
  `globals.css:1117` states the marketplace grid "avoids the same cost by pre-blurring ONE backdrop element", and the Bugs entry above about `FilesTab.tsx:393` repeats it as settled. A search during the 2026-08-07 animation audit could not find any rule implementing it: `MarketplaceScreen.tsx:281` does render `<WallpaperBackdrop/>`, but the theme-engine's `[data-wallpaper] .in-view .layer-surface` rule still matches the cards themselves (`MarketplaceCard.tsx:246`), which would mean N live blur layers, not one. Either the comment is wrong or the rule is missing — both are worth knowing, and the comment is currently load-bearing for a "don't worry about it" decision. Resolve it, then fix whichever side is wrong. Related and still genuinely open: the same question for `FilesTab.tsx:393` doc/folder cards.

- [x] **`bash scripts/ast-grep/check.sh` is RED on `master`** `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-07, **FIXED 2026-08-06/09 — youcoded `feb76640`, merged via `8c62ca51`. check.sh re-verified green 2026-08-12 (5/5 fixture rules fire, zero real-source violations).**)
  **The entry's premise was wrong: the rule was RIGHT.** `tool-bounds-not-hand-rolled` was not over-broadened and never matched a "fragment-resolution note" — it was correctly firing on two genuine hand-rolled `[body truncated at 5MB]` strings in `web-fetch.ts:160,169`. The fix (`feb76640`, "WebFetch overstated what it showed") removed the hand-rolled strings from the tool so the cap is declared via bounds; no rule carve-out was needed. The only remaining regex-shaped text in the file lives inside `//` WHY comments, which the AST rule doesn't match.

- [ ] **Resumed native sessions with no *stored* title still show `Resuming…` until their next completed turn** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-06, residue of youcoded PR #286)
  PR #286 fixed the two structural halves of the stuck-pill bug: an already-titled session now gets its stored title re-pushed on resume (`native-resume-title.ts`), and a never-titled one can finally generate a title (the `'Resuming…'` placeholder no longer fools `hasTitle`). The remaining gap is the case where the store has **no** title at all — a conversation predating the title feeder, one where all 3 generation attempts failed (offline provider), or one where `getBinding` returned null. `reapplyStoredTitle` reads only `ConversationRecord.title`, whose sole native writer is the feeder's own `onTitle`, so it finds nothing and no-ops. Meanwhile the **Resume Browser row the user just clicked shows a real name** — derived from the conversation's first user message (`harness/session-store.ts:220-228`, overlaid by the store title only when it is real, `session-browser.ts:518`). So the row reads "help me refactor the auth module" and the pill it opens reads `Resuming…`. It self-heals at the next `turn-complete`, so it only bites someone who resumes purely to re-read a transcript.
  Fix = have the re-apply fall back to that derived title. **Held because it is a copy decision, not a mechanical one**: derived titles are raw first-message text, so planting one on the pill makes the pill look different from a generated title. Decide whether that is better or worse than the placeholder before implementing. Same gap exists CC-side when the topic file has been pruned.

- [ ] **Adopt the deferred ESLint rules — 79 renderer floating promises and 43 exhaustive-deps hits are still unguarded** `bug` `#tooling` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-06, follow-up to youcoded `51d859e4`)
  The new lint gate (`desktop/eslint.config.mjs`) enables only the 21 rules already at zero, so it fails on new defects and nothing else. The rules that still fire are listed at the bottom of that file with measured counts (all / `src/main` only) so adoption can be priced rather than re-measured: `no-unnecessary-condition` 631/224, `require-await` 152/93, `no-unnecessary-type-assertion` 138/41, `return-await` 111/106, **`no-floating-promises` (renderer) 79/0**, **`exhaustive-deps` 43/0**, `no-promise-executor-return` 26/24, `require-atomic-updates` 21/13, `no-cond-assign` 14/4, `no-base-to-string` 13/10.
  **`exhaustive-deps` is the highest-value one** — those are stale-closure bugs. Caveat from this session: both security-adjacent hits I spot-checked (ToolCard's "Always allow" decision, ResumeBrowser's skip-permissions default) turned out to be *correctly* hand-maintained, so expect a high false-positive rate and adopt file-by-file rather than in one sweep. Renderer `no-floating-promises` is real but each site needs a judgement call about the right recovery, which is why it was scoped to `src/main/**` where a rejection can kill the process. Drive a rule to zero in the same commit that enables it — a gate shipping with known violations trains everyone to ignore it.

- [ ] **`desktop/tests/` (347 files) is neither type-checked nor linted** `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-06)
  `tsconfig.json`'s `include` is `src/**/*`, so `tsc --noEmit` never sees the test tree — vitest executes those files with esbuild stripping types without checking them. The new ESLint config is scoped to `src/**` for the same reason: type-aware rules need the files in a TS project. One tests tsconfig unlocks both at once. `scripts/verify.sh` already states this limitation in its header; this is the fix for it.

- [x] **`ToolCard` interpolates `unknown`-typed tool inputs straight into chat-visible strings** `bug` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-06, from the 2026-08-06 lint sweep; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #295, merge `3dac8828`.** Turned out much bigger than the three named sites: the review found the same class INCLUDED live crashes — `basename(input.path as string)` in Grep/Glob/Read/Write/Edit branches, `.replace`/`.trimStart`/`.includes` on lie-cast strings — any of which took down the whole Chat ErrorBoundary pane. Every interpolated field in `friendlyToolDisplay` now goes through an `asString()` helper or a typeof guard; 21 tests, all red-first. Twin shape in `tool-views/ToolBody.tsx` + `AskUserQuestionCard` deep fields ticketed separately below.)
  `ToolCard.tsx:99/103/120` render `input.glob`, `input.type` and `input.subagent_type` into the tool card label/detail with no string cast, while the sibling lines in the same switch DO cast (`input.path as string`, `input.pattern as string`). These are `unknown` off the tool-input JSON, so a non-string value renders as `[object Object]` in the timeline. Low severity — the models emit strings in practice — but it is a three-line fix and the inconsistency with the adjacent lines is the tell. Caught by `@typescript-eslint/no-base-to-string`, which is in the deferred list above (13 hits total).

- [ ] **Android `SessionService.kt` dereferences `bootstrap!!` in ~18 IPC handlers** `bug` `#android` (added 2026-08-06, reachability UNVERIFIED)
  Roughly 18 handlers do `File(bootstrap!!.homeDir, …)`; if any is reachable before bootstrap completes it is a hard NPE. The team is already aware of the pattern in one spot — `SessionService.kt:2537/2577` carry comments about a sign-out window making "a re-read + `!!` throw". **I could not verify whether any handler is actually reachable pre-bootstrap**: there is no Android SDK on this machine (`ANDROID_HOME` unset, no `~/Android/Sdk`), so `./gradlew lint` and `./gradlew test` both fail at SDK resolution and the 18 Kotlin test files never ran. This came from `rg` pattern-matching only — establish reachability before treating it as a real bug or before touching anything.

- [ ] **Model favourites are localStorage-only, and they are now the picker's default view** `bug` `#renderer` `#sync` (added 2026-07-31, from youcoded#279)
  `ModelPicker` opens showing favourites and nothing else until you type, which is the design Destin picked — but the favourites live in `localStorage` under `youcoded-model-favorites`, so a second device opens the picker empty with no indication why. Needs a real synced channel (the Conversation Store already syncs per-conversation state; this is per-USER state, which has no lane yet). Low severity until someone actually runs two devices, which Destin does.

- [ ] **The `bg-inset` FIELD surface is invisible on `bg-inset` hosts — patched at three call sites, not fixed** `bug` `#renderer` `#ui-consistency` (added 2026-07-31, from youcoded#279)
  `FIELD_SURFACE` is `bg-inset`, and so is every card/sheet a field gets nested in, so the control ends up exactly the colour of its own background — it reads as a label, not something you can type in. Destin reported it once (the model picker's trigger); it was then found twice more on the same branch (close prompt editor, resume tag sheet) and each was patched with a `bg-well` override at the call site. `field.ts` predicted this in a comment — "inputs on `bg-inset/50` cards now sit closer to their background than before… the alternative (bg-well inside inset cards) was offered during review and not taken." Three occurrences in one branch says the default is wrong for nested hosts rather than that N call sites each need an override. Proper fix is a decision about the primitive, not a fourth patch.

- [x] **Resume Browser takes ~1s to open and ~280ms per search keystroke — the list is not windowed** `bug` `#renderer` `#performance` (added + shipped 2026-07-31, `f8ca631b`, merged in youcoded#279)
  Destin: "it definitely does feel slow… i've been wanting to optimize it for performance for a while." Measured, not estimated, at his real scale of ~1,642 rows: **948ms** from click to committed list (**37,920 DOM nodes**), **278ms** per search keystroke; against 225ms/546 nodes/48ms with 5 rows. `ResumeBrowser.tsx:1193` maps every row on every render with no virtualization, ~23 DOM nodes per card, and `renderSessionRow` is an unmemoized closure so all 1,642 subtrees reconcile on any state change.
  **File IO is NOT the cause and is already ruled out** — the whole of `listPastSessions()` measures **0.06s** against the real `~/.claude` tree (walk+stat 0.01, tail reads 0.01, head reads 0.03, store list 0.01). An earlier suggestion to skip the transcript scrape for store-covered rows would save ~10ms of a ~1000ms problem; don't.
  **Fix (2026-07-31): chunked reveal, not virtualization.** The list renders 50 items and an IntersectionObserver sentinel tops it up by 50 as the user scrolls. Matched before/after at 1,642 rows, median of 5 runs: open **804ms → 96ms** (script 425→72, style 194→13, layout 99→8), DOM nodes **37,920 → 1,585**, keystrokes **220/319/183ms → 64/83/100ms**. Open time is now **flat in conversation count** (90ms at 5 rows, 96ms at 1,642, 85ms at 4,000).
  Two pre-measurement hypotheses were **disproved** and should not be re-derived: `React.memo` on the row could never have fixed the OPEN (a first render has nothing to reuse — all the script and style cost still happens), and the filter pipeline never needed debouncing (keystroke cost shows no trend with session count). Remaining trade-off, accepted deliberately: the scrollbar is proportional to what is revealed, and scrolling through many hundreds of rows re-accumulates DOM. True windowing is the upgrade if deep scrolling ever becomes a real usage pattern.
  Full diagnosis, the two counting traps (subagent transcripts inflate the transcript count 2.2×; all 548 browsable transcripts DO have store records), and the reproducible harness: `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-07-31-resume-browser-load-time-handoff.md`.
