- [ ] **Window-resize lag has a SECOND cause, unidentified — the inactive-session layout fix does not explain all of it** `bug` `#renderer` `#linux` `#perf` (added 2026-08-06)
  The identified cause is **fixed on master (`81c9562d`, 2026-08-06 — "perf(renderer): take inactive sessions out of layout during resize"; verified live at `ChatView.tsx:706`)**: `App.tsx` mounts a `ChatView` per open session and hid inactive ones with `visibility:hidden`, which does NOT leave the layout tree, so every resize tick re-laid-out all six of Destin's open conversations. Keying `content-visibility` on a new `sessionActive` prop measured 117 → 43 ms/step with long tasks 13 → 0, and made session switching *faster* than the behaviour it replaced (5.3 → 0.5 ms median; `display:none` would have cost 25.3 ms). Verified end-to-end in the workbench.
  **What that does not explain:** a dev window with ONE session open — zero inactive panes, so the fix is inert there — still lagged. The probe from that run showed 468 resize events, 1 long task (150 ms), and frame gaps of 230/153/94/45 ms. Big gaps with almost no blocking script means paint/raster/compositing, not layout or JS.
  **Suspects, none measured:** the 82 `backdrop-filter` sites + wallpaper layer (the earlier reduce-effects test does NOT clear these — it ran on the live app where the six-session layout cost would have masked any paint cost); 1.5× fractional scale on 2560×1600 @ 180 Hz (5.5 ms frame budget); and two always-mounted chrome paths that cost the same at one session as at six — `SessionStrip.tsx:741` (`new ResizeObserver(() => repack())`, unthrottled) and `HeaderBar.tsx` (recounted 2026-08-12: **3** `ResizeObserver`s at :121/:321/:384, not the 5-plus-MutationObserver previously written here; the cluster-reservation measure-then-write loop is still real).
  **Also unverified:** everything was measured on a Vite dev build, never the packaged app where the 1–2 s stall was actually reported. Next step is a Chromium trace with paint/raster categories over a resize sweep plus an effects-on/off A/B in the workbench; the harness for it is `scripts/resize-bench.mjs` (its header carries the probe snippet and how to read the output).

- [ ] **Audit the other repeated `.layer-surface` grids for the same per-tile blur cost** `bug` `#renderer` `#themes` (added 2026-07-31, follow-up to youcoded#277)
  #277 fixed the command drawer, where 20–40 tiles each carried their own `backdrop-filter` under `[data-wallpaper]` and Windows Electron dropped their paint (cards blinking in and out on halftone-dimension). The fix is scoped to `.command-drawer` on purpose. **Not yet checked: `FilesTab.tsx:393` doc/folder cards**, also `.layer-surface` in a grid — I never established what backdrop they sit over, so I don't know whether their blur is visible (keep it) or a no-op over an opaque parent (cancel it, as the drawer now does). No flicker reported there; the parent isn't `transform`-animating, so the likely symptom is wasted GPU rather than dropped paint. Same question applies to any future grid — the rule in `.claude/rules/react-renderer.md` → Overlays now states the test ("what's actually behind this tile?"). ~~Marketplace grid is already answered: it sits over `<WallpaperBackdrop />`, blur is visible, and it pre-blurs one element instead of N.~~ **That last claim is exactly what the "Unverified claim" entry above disputes — the two entries contradict each other, and the entry above has the evidence (`[data-wallpaper] .layer-surface` still matches the cards; the only carve-out is `.command-drawer`-scoped). Treat the marketplace grid as UNanswered until that entry resolves.**

- [ ] **Transcript storage has no long-term plan — GitHub will run out for everyone, and large transcripts are already unsyncable** `feature` `#sync` `#conversations` (added 2026-07-30, needs a design pass)
  Surfaced by the "Personal sync is large" notice on the Z13 and then measured, not estimated. **Three problems, one root:** conversation transcripts are append-only JSONL that grow without bound, and the sync system stores them as full copies in a git repo on GitHub.
  **(1) The ceiling is real and everyone hits it.** Z13's Personal space: **841 MB local / 652 MB on GitHub**, against GitHub's 1 GB soft limit and 5 GB hard limit. A single project space (`PAF 574`) is already **439 MB** on the same trajectory. This is not a power-user edge case — it is what any daily user reaches on a long enough timeline, and the failure mode at the limit is GitHub refusing pushes, i.e. sync stops for everyone on that account.
  **(2) There is NO pruning logic anywhere.** Nothing ages out, compacts, or tiers transcripts — not by size, not by date, not by device. `snapshot-retention.ts` prunes *Drive/iCloud backup snapshots* only; the sync spaces themselves grow monotonically forever. Confirmed: history compaction would NOT save this — measured on a throwaway clone, squashing away **all** 75,566 historical blob versions lands at **611 MB**, still over the 500 MiB warn threshold, because the bulk is *current content* (3,415 files / 1,892 MB uncompressed), not history. So the documented "manual compaction procedure" is not the answer and should not be reached for.
  **(3) Large transcripts are effectively unsyncable, and the UX for that lies.** `MAX_SYNC_FILE_BYTES` is 50 MB, so today everything rides; but the content is extremely concentrated — **68 transcripts (4% of files) hold 736 MB**, with nine over 20 MB and one at 43 MB — while all 1,673 conversation *records* total **1 MB**. Metadata is nearly free; transcripts are the entire cost. The obvious lever (lower the cap) is blocked by a UX truth problem: a record whose transcript is absent renders as **"Not synced to this device yet"** (`session-browser.ts:520` `fs.existsSync` probe → `notSyncedYet` → disabled row in `ResumeBrowser.tsx:670`). That wording is built for a *temporary* gap where waiting works. A size-excluded transcript would never arrive, so the message would name a false cause and imply a resolution that never comes — exactly what `docs/error-message-standards.md` forbids. Related asymmetry found in the same pass: the backend resume refusal ("This conversation hasn't synced to this device yet") exists **only** in the native lane (`ipc-handlers.ts:577`); the CC lane relies on the UI gate alone.
  **The goal to design toward: every transcript available on every device, always.** Destin's framing (2026-07-30) — GitHub may simply be the wrong permanent store for transcripts specifically, in which case options include a different backing store for transcript bytes (records keep riding git, since they're 1 MB), on-demand fetch from a peer or a blob store, or folding this into a future **YouCoded-managed cloud offered at cheap-ish rates** (which would also give the app a first-party answer to "where does my data live"). Whatever it becomes, it needs an honest not-here state and a fetch affordance, since the "yet" wording only survives if absence stays temporary. Not scoped yet — deliberately parked for a proper design pass.
  Measured during the 2026-07-27 corruption repair session; see `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-30-sync-corruption-self-heal-design.md` for the sibling work (that spec fixes silent sync failure, NOT this).

- [ ] Permission asks expire after 5 min and wedge the session silently — AskUserQuestion is the acute case `bug` `#permissions` `#desktop` `#android` (added 2026-07-30)
  Design settled: `docs/active/specs/2026-07-30-permission-ask-timeout-design.md`. A chat-view permission ask dies at ~5 min with no indication anywhere while CC is still blocked. `install-hooks.js:126` registers the hook at `timeout: 300` and `relay-blocking.js:22` uses the same `300000` ms — nominally identical, but CC's clock starts first, so **CC always wins** and kills the hook with no decision (the spec's later reviews corrected the first draft's "they race" reading). Either way the socket closes, `chat-reducer.ts:1234` flips the card to `status:'failed'`, and nothing escalates: `useAttentionClassifier` is gated on `visible` (`ChatView.tsx:200`), so a background session is never classified at all.
  **Why AskUserQuestion is the one that bites:** CC's own `askUserQuestionTimeout` ("Question auto-continue timeout", options `["never","60s","5m","10m"]`) **defaults to `never`** — verified in the CC binary at `~/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.220`. So CC waits indefinitely and our hook is the only clock; when it fires the session wedges permanently. It's also the only prompt still routed to chat in bypass mode (`main.ts:889` never auto-approves it), and the slowest to answer. Android is worse: its relay asset defaults to **120s**.
  Fix (per the spec, now through three review passes) = the app owns the clock: tier-1 app hold (magnitude open — 24h vs recommended 2h, Destin's call) < relay backstop < CC backstop, 30-min margins each (CC-wins is the bad outcome: hook killed with no decision, and AskUserQuestion then waits forever on its `never` default; a deny releasing the turn is now verified in the CC binary), plus a menu-present/menu-absent discriminator on `PERMISSION_EXPIRED` so answering in the terminal clears the card quietly instead of stamping it "expired", plus digit-rebind for single-select asks only. **Two traps found in review:** `Bootstrap.kt:992-1018` guards the whole write behind `if (!prRegistered)`, so Android existing installs would never receive the new value — changing the literal alone ships dead; and `relay-blocking.js:19-21` documents the *opposite* intent ("relay must not fire first"), so it needs a rewrite, not a renumber, or a future session reverts the margin. Also uncovered a live pre-existing hole: every stray-Enter guard (`hasPendingPermission`, `hasPendingInteraction`) goes false at expiry while CC's Ink menu is still on screen.

- [x] `McpManager` keys holders by session id, so a resumed session's release closes the connection its own re-acquire just took `bug` `#native-runtime` `#mcp` (added 2026-07-31, found in the native-MCP phase 1 final review)
  A resumed session reuses its session id. `NativeSessionHost.destroy()` releases the outgoing generation while `create()`/`resume()` acquires for the incoming one, under the same id — and `McpManager`'s holder set is keyed by that id, so it cannot tell the two generations apart. The shipped mitigation defers the close until the owning `acquire()` settles, then runs it: the incoming session is handed a live connection that closes one microtask later, and its tool calls return `"<server> is not connected."` for the rest of the session.
  That is a deliberate trade, not an oversight — the alternative (skip the close when an acquire is in flight) leaks the spawned subprocess, which three regression tests in `mcp-manager.test.ts` pin. This same bug was found and relocated three times during phase 1 before landing here; the reason it keeps moving is that the id is the wrong key.
  **Fix:** `acquire()` returns an opaque handle and `release()` takes it, so two generations of one session id are distinguishable. Comment recording the exact limitation is at `mcp-manager.ts`'s `release()`.

- [ ] Native MCP phase 2 — settings UI, the adopt flow, and IPC `feature` `#native-runtime` `#mcp` (added 2026-08-05, deferred by phase 1 which merged as youcoded#280)
  Phase 1 is **developer-operable only**: the only way to configure a server is hand-writing `~/.youcoded/mcp.json`. There is no settings UI, no way to add a server from inside the app, no adopt flow for servers already in `~/.claude.json`, and no IPC surface — so a non-technical user cannot use MCP at all today. Projection runs one way (YouCoded → Claude Code); a marketplace MCP visible in Claude Code is still invisible to native sessions.
  Carries one known Low from the phase 1 final review, unreachable until this ships: `mcp-registry.ts` `upsert()` filters with the strict `fromStored` predicate, so the first `upsert()` would permanently DELETE a hand-edited entry whose id is empty or contains `__`, where `remove()` preserves it. `upsert()` has no production caller today, which is the only reason it is harmless.
  Also deferred: folding the plugin-manifest scan into the registry, so plugin-bundled and user-configured servers share one ownership rule instead of two code paths in `mcp-reconciler.ts`.

- [x] Ten atomic-write call sites still use a FIXED `<file>.tmp`, which is not atomic against a concurrent writer `bug` `#desktop` `#reliability` (added 2026-08-05, found while merging native-MCP phase 1; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #296, merge `0a325b99`, plus workspace guard commit `e9ca14b`.** All eleven sites (the ten plus `github-client.ts` and `project-manager.ts` which the original count missed) now use pid-suffixed temp names; the review caught that pid names turn crash orphans PERMANENT, so cas-write and the two user-tree writers gained `sweepStaleTmp` (transcript-mirror's pattern) + error-path unlinks; `update-installer.ts`'s `.partial` deliberately kept fixed (load-bearing for crash recovery). The wished-for ast-grep rule EXISTS now (`atomic-tmp-name-per-process`, fires 13/13 on the old shapes, silent on fixed source). Two parked follow-ups: the `*.tmp`-in-DEFAULT_IGNORES product decision (own entry above) and optionally tightening the sweep match to `^base(\.\d+(\.\d+)?)?\.tmp$`.)
  Write-temp-then-rename protects against a crash mid-write. It does NOT protect against a second writer: both processes write the same temp path, the first rename consumes it, and the second throws `ENOENT` renaming a file that no longer exists. This is reachable in normal use — `~/.claude/` is SHARED between the installed app and a dev instance (`run-dev.sh` isolates userData and ports, not the home dir), so two YouCoded processes writing the same config file is the ordinary case, not a corner one.
  `skill-config-store.ts` was fixed on the native-MCP branch because it blocked that merge (its parallel-worker failure is the same two-writer shape). The remaining sites, verified by `rg` over `src/main` (re-verified 2026-08-12): `claude-code-registry.ts:119`, `marketplace-config-store.ts:41`, `marketplace-auth-store.ts:84`, `announcement-service.ts:33`, `artifacts/cas-write.ts:75`, `changelog-service.ts:50`, `sync-spaces/space-manager.ts:86`, `integration-installer.ts:93`, `ipc-handlers.ts:3717` (was :3576) — **plus two sites the original count missed: `github-client.ts:217` and `artifacts/project-manager.ts:67-68`**, so eleven total, not ten.
  The fix is mechanical and the convention already exists in this codebase — `mcp-reconciler.ts`, `hook-reconciler.ts`, `saved-folders.ts` and `transcript-mirror.ts` all use `${path}.${process.pid}.tmp`. Worth an ast-grep rule so the fixed-`.tmp` shape can't come back.

- [x] `window-all-closed` is the app's ONLY quit hook — Cmd+Q, dock quit and SIGTERM skip every teardown `bug` `#desktop` `#lifecycle` (added 2026-07-30, found in the native-MCP phase 1 Task 4 review)
  `main.ts` registers exactly one quit-related listener: `app.on('window-all-closed')` (`main.ts:1915-1942`), which calls `ipc-handlers.ts`'s `cleanup()`. There is **no** `before-quit`, `will-quit`, `quit` or `SIGTERM` handler anywhere in the file — verified by a repo-wide search during the Task 4 review, not inferred. So any quit route that does not go through window-all-closed first runs none of the teardown: macOS Cmd+Q and dock-quit call `app.quit()` directly (firing `before-quit`/`will-quit`/`quit`), and an OS-shutdown/logout SIGTERM bypasses Electron's quit events entirely on every platform.
  **What leaks:** `sessionManager.destroyAll()`, `hookRelay.stop()`, and — as of the native-MCP branch — `mcpManager.destroyAll()`, whose whole purpose is keeping a stdio MCP server's spawned subprocess from outliving the app. An MCP subprocess surviving a Cmd+Q is a real user-facing bug, not a theoretical one, and it is the exact failure the MCP teardown step was written to prevent.
  Pre-existing and app-wide, so deliberately NOT patched inside the MCP branch — the MCP teardown correctly hooks in beside the others. Fix is a `before-quit`/`will-quit` handler (idempotent with the existing one, since both can fire) plus a SIGTERM handler; `native-session-host.ts`'s `destroyAll()` carries a comment recording the exact scope limit so a future reader does not mistake the hook for full coverage.

- [ ] Buddy chat's input bar never consults the PTY gate at all — a typed message can land in a live Ink menu `bug` `#buddy` `#pty-io` (added 2026-07-31, found by the permission-ask-timeout final review)
  `components/buddy/BuddyChat.tsx:178` (path corrected 2026-08-12) renders `<InputBar sessionId={viewedSession} compact />` without `getSessionState`, `onSendBlocked`, or `onToast`. `InputBar.sendMessage`'s gate check needs `getSessionState` to resolve a session, so with it absent the whole guarded block — refusal, toast, and "Send anyway" affordance — never executes in buddy chat. A message typed there while a permission/AskUserQuestion/plan menu is live goes straight to the PTY, and its trailing `\r` confirms the highlighted option. Same class as youcoded#110, which the gate was built to close on the main input bar. Pre-existing and untouched by the timeout branch, but that branch makes `awaiting-approval` states long-lived (a 2h hold instead of a 5-min expiry), which widens the window this is exposed in. Fix is wiring the three props; the harder question is what the buddy overlay's refusal UI should look like at 320px.

- [ ] `HookRelay` can drop a permission expiry entirely in one narrow ordering `bug` `#permissions` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-31, found by the permission-ask-timeout final review)
  If the tier-1 hold fires while the socket is already `destroyed` but its `'close'` event has not yet dispatched, `respond()` deletes the pending entry and returns false (so the hold correctly does not claim an auto-deny), and then the `'close'` handler's `wasOpen` guard finds the entry gone and also stays silent — zero expiry events for that request. The card keeps rendering as a live ask with a dead socket. It degrades honestly rather than wedging: clicking any button gets `delivered === false`, which dispatches `PERMISSION_EXPIRED` with `reason: 'delivery-failed'` and resolves the card with accurate copy. Narrow, and not introduced by the timeout branch, but the cleanest fix is for desktop's `respond()` to emit `'delivery-failed'` itself on the not-delivered path the way Android's does, instead of leaving that recovery only in the renderer.

- [ ] `HookRelay.closeSocket()` has zero call sites `bug` `#permissions` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-31, found by the permission-ask-timeout final review)
  Dead code, and knip does not flag it because it is an exported class method. It is symmetric with Android's `EventBridge.closeSocket()`, which also has no callers — so either both are a deliberate API surface for a cross-path cleanup that was never wired, or both should go. The timeout branch kept it correct (it clears its hold timer) rather than deleting it mid-feature.

- [ ] Plan-approval buttons send arrows+Enter in ONE write — the shape measured broken on CC 2.1.220 `bug` `#desktop` `#pty-io` (added 2026-07-30, found during the permission-ask-timeout spec's third review)
  `ToolCard.tsx:472-473` (`PlanApprovalButtons`) sends `DOWN.repeat(optionIndex) + '\r'` as a single `sendInput` — exactly what `.claude/rules/pty-io.md` forbids and what `ink-select-parser.ts:345-351` measured on CC 2.1.220 (2026-07-26, the Resume Session bug root cause): CC drops arrows sharing a write with the Enter and confirms the HIGHLIGHTED option. If that measurement holds here, every plan-approval click commits option 1 regardless of which button was pressed. Needs a quick dev-instance check to confirm, then the `input`/`submitInput` two-write split. Independent of the permission-timeout redesign — fixable on its own.

- [x] Desktop app burns CPU continuously at idle — measure, attribute, fix `bug` `#perf` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-30, **fixed 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #275; PR #274 closed, premise disproven**)
  Root cause was **not** the lease/heartbeat lead (renderer JS 99% idle, `pty:output` 0 bytes). It is a platform property: **on a high-refresh display, ANY smoothly-animating element makes Chromium present a frame per refresh at ~1.5–1.9ms of CPU each — ~29% of one core at 180Hz — regardless of element size or property.** Not app-specific: the identical pulsing div measured 29.8% in YouCoded, **33.7% in a bare Electron window with zero app code, and 26.7% in plain Google Chrome**. Cost is per-FRAME, not per-element (8 divs ≈ 1 div; an 8px layer-promoted transform animation still cost 31.9%).
  **Correction trail:** PR #274 shipped `will-change` layer promotion claiming 12× (43% → 3.6%); re-measurement disproved it (29.8% → 27.8%) — the original "promoted" reading was taken against a freshly-restored window that was not yet presenting (same artifact as the acknowledged bogus 0 fps reading). An early steps() test was likewise contaminated by probe-window occlusion. Lesson: **window presentation state is part of any perf experiment — re-run the baseline in the same window state before trusting an A/B.**
  The levers that actually work, both shipped in **PR #275**: (1) **fewer frames** — `steps()` timing on decorative animations (smooth 28.75% → `steps(8)` 9.33%): `.animate-pulse`/breathe/challenge-pulse at steps(8) (visually indistinguishable), `.animate-spin` at steps(12) (deliberate 12-tick spinner look). This is the per-animation form of Destin's "cap it to 30fps" suggestion — which was directionally right; there is no app-global knob (Electron `setFrameRate` is offscreen-only, Chromium switches only remove caps). (2) **zero frames** — Reduced Effects now actually stops CSS animations (`data-reduced-effects` previously had ZERO css rules gating on it; 48.78% → 2.44%), and `.flowing-word` is finite (plays twice, settles — its `background-position` paint is main-thread, immune to both promotion and steps()). Original 35.6% repro scenario: now **11.0%** with the animation running, ~2.5% with none.
  Also corrected from the handoff: the 104–133% live-app readings were **not idle** (the measuring session ran inside the app alongside 6 other streaming CC sessions ≈ 99% of a core); hidden windows were always fine (Chromium throttles them, 3.9% minimized) — the problem is exclusively visible-and-idle. 180Hz machines pay 3× a 60Hz laptop.
  Guards: `desktop/tests/animation-frame-budget.test.ts` (pins every quantization + bans new inline smooth-infinite animations in components/), `desktop/tests/reduced-effects-animations.test.ts`, `scripts/measure-idle-cpu.mjs` (flags SMOOTH animations vs `frame-budgeted: steps(N)`; refuses to sample the packaged app). Write-up incl. full correction history: docs/archive/investigations/2026-07-30-idle-cpu-burn.md.
  Follow-ups: ~~`model-load-sweep` animates `left`~~ and ~~mascot/buddy SVG keyframes~~ **both closed 2026-08-07** (branch `perf/transcript-hover-frames`) — the sweep now uses `translateX` (no layout pass), and the mascot's five rig loops plus its 33ms limb interval now pause on `visibilitychange` rather than being quantized, since `steps()` on character motion reads as juddering. `buddy-breathe` is deliberately NOT fixed: the buddy window is `alwaysOnTop`, so `visibilitychange` never fires, and pausing breathing makes the character look dead — recorded as an accepted cost in `ANIMATION_EXCEPTIONS` (`animation-frame-budget.test.ts`); ~~three full-rate rAF loops~~ (all fixed in PR #275 — BrailleSpinner/ThemeEffects on intervals, MascotRig dual-rate with rAF only while dragging; rig themes were presenting 180 sway frames/sec since idleSway never lets springs park); a session that never initializes still shows a permanent "Initializing session…" overlay (UX bug, no longer a CPU one); and whether this machine's per-frame cost is inflated by the missing `DC_DISABLE_IPS` amdgpu flag (measure after the kernel-param fix, or at 60Hz).

- [ ] verify.sh related-tests fail in symlinked-node_modules worktrees — Vite "Denied ID …?inline" `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-07-30; **WORKAROUND PROVEN 2026-08-12: replace the symlink with a hardlink farm — `cp -al youcoded/desktop/node_modules <worktree>/desktop/node_modules` — realpaths then stay inside the worktree root and Vite stops denying; verified in the ToolCard PR #295 worktree where the symlink failed 5 test files and `cp -al` passed everything. A real fix in vitest.config.ts `server.fs.allow` would still be better.**)
  On Linux, worktrees share the main checkout's `desktop/node_modules` via a symlink (mirroring the Windows junction convention). Vitest resolves `highlight.js/styles/github-dark.css?inline` through the symlink to its REAL path under `youcoded/desktop/node_modules`, which sits outside the worktree's Vite `server.fs` allow-list → "Denied ID", failing every test file that imports theme-context — while the identical file passes on the main checkout. Confirmed environmental 2026-07-30 (fails with the working diff stashed). Bites any worktree session whose diff touches a file whose related tests import theme-context; CI is unaffected (real node_modules). Likely fix: add the workspace root (or `searchForWorkspaceRoot`) to `server.fs.allow` in `desktop/vitest.config.ts` — note verify.sh auto-escalates to the full suite when vitest.config.ts changes, so land it as its own tiny PR.

- [x] `.flowing-word` keyword gradient burns ~40% of a core whenever a keyword is on screen `bug` `#perf` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-30, **fixed 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #275**)
  `.flowing-word` (used by `FlowingKeywords.tsx` + `UserMessage.tsx`) animated `background-position` on an infinite loop — main-thread painted, so **no rendering trick helps**: plain 43.8%, `will-change: background-position` 43.0%, forced layer via `will-change: transform` 65.9% (vs 66.1% unpromoted), `steps(24)` still ~40%. Fixed by making it **finite**: plays twice on appearance (~6s of flow, preserving the draw-the-eye purpose), then settles to the static start gradient — screenshot-verified legible. Cost after settling: zero. Reduced Effects stops it entirely. Guarded by `animation-frame-budget.test.ts` (`infinite` on this rule fails the suite). The fancier alternative (transform-driven gradient overlay behind `background-clip: text`) is no longer needed for perf; only worth revisiting if Destin wants the endless-flow look back.

- [x] Sync transport converts git failures into success — a crash-corrupted repo syncs "green" forever `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, **MERGED 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #276, merge 5a787879**; spec + plan archived to `docs/archive/`)
  Found live on the Z13: three hard freezes (Jul 27–29, the EC-wedge machine) left 16 zero-byte loose objects in the Personal space's `sync.git`; `refs/heads/main` pointed at one of them, so every git op failed `fatal: bad object HEAD` — and **sync was dead for three days while the panel showed green and `lastSync` advanced every cycle** (3,381 files staged, never pushed). Two defects: (1) `git-transport.ts` `git()` never throws and `push()`/`pull()` map failures to `{pushed:false}`/`{updated:false}` — indistinguishable from "nothing to do" — so the engine emits phantom `synced` events that stamp `recordSyncSuccess` (verified by reproducing the transport's exact command sequence against the corrupt repo: commit exits 128 → silent benign return). Only auth failures deliberately surface; every other mode falls into the trap `classifyGitAuthFailure`'s own WHY comment warns about. (2) Zero-byte objects are *poison*: git checks existence by filename, so `add -A` keeps "succeeding" and never rewrites them from the intact worktree — corruption never self-heals. Related third defect, same session (screenshot): the devices-list **self** row reads legacy `.sync-marker` (absent on GitHub-era installs) → "last seen 22 hours ago" on a running machine, while peer rows ride the live SyncHub map; `syncInProgress` stats the legacy `.sync-lock` identically. Approved design (all four parts): benign-allowlist inversion (`classifyGitFailure` — non-zero exit ⇒ error unless allowlisted; corrupt patterns throw coded `repo-corrupt`), two-tier auto-repair (surgical zero-byte purge + reset to `origin/main`, escalate to backup-aside + refetch; once per space per launch; worktree never touched), notice/danger surfacing via existing panel plumbing, and the self-row rewire onto `lastSyncFor` evidence. Sequencing: land PR #248 (same legacy-state family, open since 07-24) first, then honesty before the row rewire — the row is only as truthful as the events feeding it. Z13's own repo to be repaired by hand in the same session (local history keepable — `ca2f6a0c` verified intact; pending app quit).

- [ ] Sync recency counts loop iterations, not contact with the remote `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review)
  `recordSyncSuccess` stamps `lastSync` on any *completed* cycle, and offline is silent by design — so an offline `push()` fails, the recovery `pull()`'s fetch fails silently, the retry push fails, `isNetworkFailureStderr` returns true, nothing throws, and the engine emits `synced` anyway. A device offline for three days polls every 120s and stamps recency every cycle; PR #276 newly surfaces that value on the devices-list **self** row, which then reads "Synced just now" while offline. Not a regression (these semantics predate the branch and the settings row has consumed them since 2026-07-22) — but it is the one place #276's honesty thesis doesn't reach, and #276 corrected `getSelfLastSyncEpochMs`'s docstring rather than the behavior. Fix: stamp recency only when the cycle actually reached the remote (fetch/push exit 0), so "Last synced" means contact. Fold in the related semantics question while there: `getSelfLastSyncEpochMs` takes the **max** across spaces, so one healthy space and two broken ones still reports "just synced". Drift risk to close at the same time — self recency is now derived twice with the same rule, in `service.getSelfLastSyncEpochMs` and `SyncPanel.tsx:380-381`; they agree today by coincidence, not construction.

- [x] Renderer maps neither `repo-corrupt` nor `repo-repair-failed`, and its fallback copy is wrong for one of them `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #299, merge `a8b0a290`**: both codes now have explicit mappings with restart-based copy whose every claim the review traced to code — repair writes only under `.youcoded/`, `healedSpaces` is in-memory so restart genuinely retries, and the offline-corrupt corner (separate entry below) surfaces different copy via a different path so nothing overpromises.)
  `summarizeSpaceSyncError` (`sync-space-error-summary.ts`) special-cases only `'github-auth'`, so both error codes PR #276 introduced fall to the generic tail: *"Sync hit an unexpected problem. It will keep retrying automatically."* For `repo-corrupt` that is merely vague. For `repo-repair-failed` it is **reassuring and wrong within the launch** — the sync does keep retrying, but the heal is guarded to one attempt per space per launch and will not run again. Accurate copy is closer to "Sync couldn't repair itself — restart the app to try again." Renderer work was explicitly out of #276's scope; this is the highest-priority of its follow-ups. Both codes are exported constants (`REPO_CORRUPT_ERROR_CODE`, `REPO_REPAIR_FAILED_ERROR_CODE`) — match on those, never on prose.

- [ ] A corrupt sync repo that is *also* offline never heals, though Tier 2 is offline-capable `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review)
  `hasRemote()` runs `git remote get-url origin` and returns `code === 0` with no corruption guard, so a repo damaged badly enough that git refuses to open it (zero-byte `HEAD`, truncated `config`) reads as "no remote". `engine.syncSpace` then takes the `ensureProvisioned` branch **before** any guarded op runs. Online this is only wasted REST work — `setRemote` fails, `pull()`'s `add -A` then throws `repo-corrupt` and the heal proceeds. Offline or signed out, `ensureProvisioned` throws first ("Not connected to GitHub…"), the corruption is never classified, and `repair()` never runs — even though Tier 2 (move aside + re-init) needs no network at all. The user sees an auth/network error for a corrupt repo, every cycle, until they reconnect. Fix: `throwIfCorrupt` inside `hasRemote()`, or a corruption probe before the `ensureProvisioned` branch.

- [x] `repair()` leaves no trace of which tier ran `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #299, merge `a8b0a290`**: `repair()` returns + logs a `RepairOutcome` {tier, zeroByteObjectsDeleted, tier1Failure, backupPath} via the subsystem's injected logFn; git/fs sequence verified byte-identical. Known residual asymmetry, deliberate: a Tier-2 *failure* exits before the tier-2 log line — the engine's "Sync self-repair failed" error event still carries the cause.)
  On crash-prone hardware "did the heal fire, and which one" is the first question a debug session asks, and nothing on disk answers it: `deleteZeroByteObjects`'s return count is discarded, Tier 1 and Tier 2 emit the **identical** notice, and the only durable artifact is the `.broken-*` directory, which `pruneBrokenBackups` reduces to one. If a user later reports "my sync history changed", there is no record. `service.ts` already has a `log` in scope at engine construction — one line per tier closes it. Smaller items in the same file while there: `tests/sync-spaces-repair.test.ts`'s `cleanup()` is not in a `finally`/`afterEach`, so a failing assertion leaks a temp git repo; `brokenBackupName` has one-second resolution, so two repairs of the same space within one second `ENOTEMPTY` the rename (non-destructive — surfaces as `repo-repair-failed`); and `getSelfLastSyncEpochMs` does a `readdir` plus one uncached `readFileSync` + `JSON.parse` **per space** on every 10s status tick (`SpaceManager.read()` is uncached), which wants a single read + max.

- [ ] Every `tsc`-emitted file ships inside `app.asar`, tests included `bug` `#build` (added 2026-07-29, measured while proving the UI workbench tree-shakes)
  `npm run build` runs `tsc` over the whole `src/` tree into `dist/`, then `vite build` produces the bundle the renderer actually loads (`dist/renderer/assets/`). **Correction 2026-08-12: the electron-builder config is `desktop/electron-builder.yml`, not a `package.json` `build` key — and it DOES have a `files:` allowlist; it's just `dist/**/*`, so the fix is narrowing that yml entry (or narrowing `tsconfig` `include` from `src/**/*` to `src/main/`), not adding a missing field.** `electron-builder` packs all of `dist/` — measured on the 1.2.4 Linux build (re-measured 2026-08-12: now **47**): **44 `.test.js` files** (`dist/main/__tests__/*`, `dist/main/analytics-service.test.js`, …) plus **19 `dist/renderer/dev/workbench/*.js`** files, including `fixture-loader.test.js`. None of it is reachable: `index.html` loads only `assets/`, main loads `dist/main/main.js`, and a grep of the entry chunk for `dev/workbench` returns 0. So this is installer weight and a slightly larger attack surface for anyone unpacking the asar, not executable dead code — which is why it is a `bug` and not a release blocker. Fix is a `build.files` allowlist (or a `tsc` output narrowed to `src/main/`), then re-measure the asar file count. Pre-dates the workbench; the workbench made it 19 files worse and is what surfaced it.

- [ ] Android `EventBridge` maps session ids with no gate at all `bug` `#android-runtime` (added 2026-07-26, found while fixing the desktop wrong-transcript bug)
  `EventBridge.kt:96-98` writes `sessionIdMap[mobileSessionId] = claudeSessionId` on **every** hook event — no `hook_event_name` check, no `source` check, no equivalent of desktop's `resolveMappingAction`. That is strictly looser than desktop's behavior *before* youcoded PR #257: a subagent or tool hook carrying a child session id repoints the mapping, which is exactly the poisoning the desktop gate exists to prevent. Exposure differs — Android reads `transcript_path` straight off the hook payload (`EventBridge.kt:101-104`) rather than reconstructing `<slug>/<id>.jsonl` from the mapping, so the desktop symptom (an unrelated conversation replaying into chat view) may not reproduce identically. Port `resolveMappingAction`'s semantics (first-sighting adopt, same-id ignore, SessionStart-only remap, refuse `startup` on an already-mapped session) and verify on a device before assuming parity. Deliberately NOT changed in #257 — the desktop fix was verified against real process state, and porting blind to a platform I couldn't test would have been guessing.
- [ ] `CLAUDE_DESKTOP_SESSION_ID` leaks into every PTY descendant `bug` `#ipc` (added 2026-07-26, root cause behind the wrong-transcript bug)
  Verified live on the Z13: PID 126721 (a `run-dev.sh` shell spawned from a Bash tool) carried `CLAUDE_DESKTOP_SESSION_ID=ba1ad72e…` and `CLAUDE_DESKTOP_PIPE=/tmp/claude-desktop-hooks-5879.sock` — both inherited verbatim from the CC session that spawned it (PID 7125). So **any** `claude` invoked from inside a session (Bash tool, script, background job) announces its hooks to the built app's relay under the *parent's* desktop session id. That is what let a foreign `SessionStart` repoint a live chat view. `pty-worker.js:241-257` already scrubs CC's own identity vars at spawn and `run-dev.sh:151-153` unsets both, so the hazard class is known — but there is no clean scrub for Bash-tool children, because the session's own hooks need those vars and the tool runs as a child of the same CC process. youcoded PR #257 guards the *consequence* (`source`-gated remap) rather than the leak. A real fix likely means a per-invocation nonce the relay can validate, so an inherited id alone isn't sufficient to claim a desktop session. Worth doing before something else keys off that env var.
- [ ] Transcript replay has no size ceiling `bug` `#chat-reducer` (added 2026-07-26, blast radius of the wrong-transcript bug)
  `TranscriptWatcher.startWatching` begins at `offset: 0`, so any (re)point at an existing JSONL re-emits the entire file as live transcript events. In the 2026-07-26 incident that meant **44 MB / 15,554 lines** parsed and dispatched into the chat reducer in one burst — and the same path also drove a conversation-store `lastActive` upsert and a mirror write for a conversation nobody had opened. Even when the remap is *correct* (a legitimate `/clear` or in-session `/resume`), nothing bounds the work. Worth a cap or a chunked/streamed replay with a progress state. Related surface worth auditing at the same time: `App.tsx:2337` seeds chat on resume via `session.loadHistory(claudeSessionId, projectSlug, 10, false)`, and `HistoryExpandButton` (`ChatView.tsx:54-62`) expands it further from `resumeInfo` — both inject history keyed by an explicitly-passed id rather than by the watcher, so they are a second, independent way for a wrong id to put the wrong conversation on screen.
- [ ] Conversation-store title and topic file disagree for the same conversation `bug` `#conversations` (added 2026-07-26, observed while diagnosing the wrong-transcript bug)
  Observed on `ff2e7ceb-c564-4e7f-81ff-59b2085d0409`: the store record (`~/YouCoded/Personal/Conversations/claude/<id>.json`) read `"title": "Fix Ink Menu Keystrokes"` while `~/.claude/topics/topic-<id>` read `Resume Session Menu Broken`. **Re-checked 2026-08-12: the cited pair now AGREES (both "Fix Ink Menu Keystrokes") with no code change — the drift converged on its own, so a fresh repro is needed before any fix.** Both are live sources for the Resume Browser's name precedence (topic file > conversation-index topic > derived-from-first-user-message, per `youcoded/docs/conversations.md`). **Not confirmed to be a defect** — the auto-title hook rewrites the topic file as a conversation's subject drifts, and `store-core.ts` merge rules treat a non-`'Untitled'` title as local truth, so the store legitimately holds an older title. The open question is whether the store is ever *meant* to follow a retitle, and which one a user should see. Investigate before assuming a fix; the risk of "fixing" it wrongly is a title fan-out that clobbers good titles across devices.
- [x] Sound presets cannot be auditioned without assigning them `bug` `#ui` (added 2026-07-26, **SHIPPED 2026-07-26** on `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1`, unmerged)
  `PresetSelector` (`SettingsPanel.tsx:340-351`) fires `onSelect(p.id); playPreview(p.id)` in the same handler, so **the only way to hear a stock sound is to make it your notification sound.** Shopping through the library means clobbering the setting 15 times and then remembering which one you wanted. Compounding it: all **15** presets (`utils/sounds.ts:133-149`) render as `px-2 py-1 rounded text-[10px]` chips in a `flex-wrap`, **twice** — once per category — so the list is both unreadable and duplicated. Fix is in the menu-internals spec §4: one library listed once, a K3 segmented control picking *which slot you are filling* (Needs attention / Response ready), a Radio to assign, and a separate `▶` icon button to audition. Optional polish in the same edit: `SoundPreset` gains a `desc` field and the tone signatures that already exist as trailing comments on `STOCK_PRESETS` ("C5 → E5", "A4 double tap") lift into it — strings unchanged; without it the 15 rows are hard to tell apart. Small and self-contained; does not need the rest of the design system to land first.
  **Outcome (2026-07-26):** shipped, but NOT as specced. Destin reviewed the first fix — Radio to assign, separate `▶` to audition — and reversed it: with one shared list behind a category toggle, assigning IS how you listen, so a second control was just another thing to aim at. Selecting a sound now plays it, and the whole tile is the hit target. `desc` shipped. The guard test inverted accordingly (`tests/sound-preview.test.ts` asserts selecting DOES preview). Also fixed in the same pass: the popup could not scroll at all, which turned out to be a `SettingsPopup` defect affecting Session Defaults too — the trigger for the whole D1 shell rework.
- [x] Settings "Backup & Sync" row freezes on launch-time warnings for the whole app run `bug` `#sync` `#ui` (added 2026-07-26, reported by Destin from his live app, **SHIPPED 2026-07-26 — youcoded PR #254, merge `6645db83`**)
  Symptom: the Settings row reads red **"Sync Failing"** with a `2` badge while the Backup & Sync popup two clicks away reads green **"All synced"** and shows no Warnings section — and `~/.claude/.sync-warnings.json` does not exist on disk. Root cause is a refresh gap, not a disagreement between the two derivations (both correctly route through `deriveSyncBoxState`/`deriveSettingsRowState`). `SyncSection` (`SyncPanel.tsx:272`) is mounted from **app launch**, not from settings-open — `DesktopSettings` renders unconditionally inside the always-mounted, translate-hidden drawer (`SettingsPanel.tsx:242,270`) — and it calls `getStatus()` exactly once, 350 ms in. Nothing ever refetches `status.warnings` after that: its `status:data` handler deliberately patches only `lastSyncEpoch`/`syncInProgress`/`backupMeta`/`lastSyncByDevice` (`SyncPanel.tsx:296-315`), and the only other caller — the popup's `refreshStatus` → `onRefresh` — auto-fires solely when the LEGACY `toolkit-state/.sync-marker` epoch advances, a file that does not exist on a spaces-only install, so `advanced` is false forever. Meanwhile `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` runs ~35 s after launch (the `rclone lsd` probe is slow — Z13 log: start `12:40:06`, `Auto-detected sync backend: drive` at `12:40:41`) and rewrites the warnings file, sweeping every stale health code. So the row reads the file as the PREVIOUS session left it and then keeps showing that snapshot for the rest of the app's uptime, while every fresh-reading surface (StatusBar pill off the 10 s `status:data` push, the popup off its own `getStatus()`) correctly shows nothing. The 07-25 22:06 launch logged no auto-detect line → `autoDetectBackend()` returned null → the phantom pair is almost certainly `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED` + `OFFLINE`, both danger, both health codes, both swept at 12:40:41 — i.e. the ROADMAP entry below (PR #248) is the SOURCE of the bogus warnings and this entry is why they outlive their own fix by a full app session. Fix: have `SyncSection` take warnings from the authoritative 10 s push (`statusData.syncWarnings`, already read straight off `.sync-warnings.json` by `buildStatusData`, `ipc-handlers.ts:1884`) instead of its frozen mount-time snapshot — App.tsx's gear danger-dot already does exactly this (`App.tsx:1823-1845`). Landing #248 alone does NOT close this: any transient warning (a real OFFLINE at launch) still pins the row red until restart.
- [ ] Native turn teardown leaves an unhandled promise rejection when a turn is aborted mid-stream `bug` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-25)
  Surfaced while rebasing Plan C, but **the defect is on master, not on that branch** — attribution was proven, not assumed: checking out master's `harness-session.ts` verbatim and adding a single `await Promise.resolve()` at the top of `turnLoop` reproduces it exactly, with no other change. Plan C's `await this.maybeCompact(...)` sits in that same position, so it shifts the microtask timing enough to make a latent race observable; `maybeCompact` itself is innocent (it returns `'none'` immediately for a small history, and a bare `await Promise.resolve()` in its place reproduces the rejection identically). Repro: `npx vitest run tests/harness-session.test.ts -t serialize` — the existing "send() while a turn is in flight rejects (callers must serialize)" test, which builds a never-closing `ReadableStream`, leaves turn 1 in flight, then calls `interrupt()`. Vitest reports `Errors 1` / `{ type: 'Unhandled Rejection', message: undefined, stacks: [] }` while all assertions still pass. **The rejection reason is literally `undefined`**, so there is no stack and no message to trace. Investigation ruled out: the SDK's terminal promises (swallowing `usage`/`finishReason`/`text`/`totalUsage`/`steps`/`response`/… in the abort branch does NOT fix it), any userland `new Promise` executor or `Promise.reject` call (a Promise-subclass wrapper that traces every `undefined` rejection never fires), and `async_hooks` promise-creation capture (the rejected promise isn't the one the init hook sees). That points at native stream/iterator machinery — most likely the cancellation path when `iterator.return()` tears down a reader that has a permanently-pending `read()`. Note the `'stall'` branch already swallows four promises with the comment *"Cancelling the reader can reject the terminal promises with nothing awaiting them"*, while the `'aborted'` branch swallows only two — that asymmetry is suspicious and worth revisiting even though widening it alone did not fix this instance. Low user impact (an `undefined` rejection carries no error detail to mask, and every assertion passes) but it dirties the suite's error count and could hide a *real* rejection later, so it should not ride along indefinitely.
- [x] "No sync configured" fires on a machine whose GitHub sync is healthy `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-24, **MERGED 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #248, merge 0a5b15b9**)
  Found by Destin on a fresh macOS install running `1.3.0-beta.9`: red "Sync Failing" StatusBar chip + non-dismissible "No sync configured — your backups aren't set up", with GitHub sync set up, connected and green two clicks away. Root cause: `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` only reads the LEGACY additional-backup backends (`storage_backends` in `toolkit-state/config.json` — Drive/iCloud/rclone). Sync spaces keeps its own state at `toolkit-state/sync-spaces.json` and is invisible to that read, so zero legacy backends = "no sync" regardless of the primary system. **Clean installs are exactly the affected population, which is why this survived to beta.9:** `autoDetectBackend()` masks it on old machines — its `rclone lsd gdrive:Claude/Backup/` probe still exits 0 on a box that used the legacy backups years ago, silently skipping the warning without enabling any backend (verified on the Z13: `storage_backends` is `[]`, rclone exits 0, no warnings file on disk). New machines have no rclone and no `~/Library/…/Claude/Backup`, so they take the warning. Fix gates the warning on the primary system, rewords `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED` off "connect a cloud provider", and renames `PERSONAL_STALE` to name the Drive/iCloud markers it actually tracks. Health codes are re-cleared every launch, so affected installs self-heal on the next start. Flip to `[x]` on merge.
- [ ] Artifact tracker writes RELATIVE paths into `absolutePath` (documented canonical-absolute) `bug` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-23)
  Found auditing the real `.youcoded/artifacts.json`: ≥7 external records (re-counted 2026-08-12: now **15**, still accumulating — new ones from the Aug work) have a non-absolute `absolutePath` — `docs/MAP.md`, `flappy-bird/styles.css`, `youcoded/desktop/src/renderer/state/chat-reducer.ts`, etc. The `ArtifactRecord.absolutePath` field is documented "canonical, set when kind='external'" (`shared/artifacts/types.ts`), and `visible-artifacts.ts`/`import-file` canonicalize it as absolute. A relative value only "resolves" by luck against the current cwd — `fs.access('docs/MAP.md')` passes when the app's cwd happens to be the workspace root, and fails everywhere else, so these records flip between present and orphaned by cwd. Something in the tracker (App.tsx artifact-tracker path, likely a Write/Edit tool-use whose path arrives project-relative) is storing the raw path instead of resolving it against the project root first. Latent regardless of the Files-tab work; surfaced by it. (found 2026-07-23)
- [ ] Remote: "+ Add file" uploads the file to the host, then the import fails `bug` `#remote` `#project-view` (added 2026-07-23)
  In remote mode `dialog.openFile()` maps to `pickAndUploadFiles()` (`remote-shim.ts:1013`) — it uploads the picked file to the desktop host and returns a host path. The renderer then calls `artifacts:import-file`, which `remote-server.ts` does NOT bridge (its only `artifacts:*` case is `list-projects-index`), so the import fails after the upload already happened. **Not a regression** — `includeExternal` was equally unbridged, so the button was already broken this way; but it now means a filesystem mutation rather than a sidecar pin, so it belongs with the existing "which namespaces are safe to expose over a password-auth'd but NOT TLS-encrypted channel" scoping decision under the remote-bridging entry. Either bridge it deliberately or hide the button in remote mode. (found 2026-07-23 auditing the Files-tab merge)
- [ ] `tests/ipc-handlers.test.ts` flakes at import time under full-suite runs `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-23)
  Fails intermittently inside `SkillConfigStore.migrate` → `skill-provider.ts:600` (was :597) → `main.ts:183` during `npm test`, and passes both in isolation and on a clean full re-run. Reproduced twice across one session on an untouched import chain. Looks like a tmp-dir/ordering race in the migration, not a real regression — but an intermittently-red suite trains people to re-run instead of read. (found 2026-07-23 during the Files-tab merge)
- [ ] Takeover holder can't detect lease loss while the SyncHub is down `bug` `#sync` `#leases` (added 2026-07-23)
  Found during the M2 dev repro (instrumented, confirmed from logs): lease *presence* has two transports (hub + the `~/YouCoded/Personal/Leases/` file fallback) but loss-detection lives only in the hub renew branch. A holder in file-fallback mode keeps optimistically renewing forever — after a force-takeover it never interrupts, never flushes, never shows the moved pill, and keeps rewriting the lease file (two installs ping-pong it). Pre-existing, provider-agnostic (CC + native). The misleading "isn't responding" dialog copy is being fixed on the M2 branch (PR #212); this entry is the deeper holder-side gap.

- [ ] Same-machine takeover handoff without the hub (lease-file signaling) `idea` `#sync` `#leases` (added 2026-07-23)
  Two installs sharing `~/YouCoded` (dev instance + built app — the sanctioned dogfood config) can see each other's lease files but can't deliver a takeover request when the hub is down (the request has exactly one transport: the SyncHub socket). A file-based request signal (holder watches its lease file's dir) would make same-machine handoff work hub-less. Nice-to-have; the hub path is primary.

- [ ] Remote WS set-tag/set-note don't broadcast `SESSION_META_CHANGED` on success `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-23)
  Found in M2 final review (youcoded PR #212): the desktop IPC surface's meta-write path broadcasts `SESSION_META_CHANGED` after a successful persist (the M2 broadcast-after-persist fix), but the remote WS `set-tag`/`set-note` handlers persist without the matching broadcast — a second remote client (or the same session viewed from another device) goes stale until its next full refresh. Pre-existing gap, not introduced by M2; M2 only fixed the desktop-IPC honesty half. **Partial adjacent work landed since (re-checked 2026-08-12): both handlers now call `emitConversationMetaChanged()` (the chatsearch signal) — but still no `remoteServer.broadcast({type: SESSION_META_CHANGED})`, which exists only on the IPC path (`ipc-handlers.ts:3067, 3089`).**

- [ ] `NativeSessionHost` needs a `quiescing` refuse-flag to close the one-macrotask takeover race `idea` `#native-runtime` `#sync` (added 2026-07-23)
  Found in M2 final review: `quiesce()` (cross-device takeover teardown) clears the send queue synchronously, then awaits one macrotask before aborting — deliberate, so a `send()` issued in the same tick can finish its deferred dispatch. But a `send()` that arrives DURING that macrotask window is not refused; it runs one full uninterrupted turn before the handoff proceeds. A `quiescing` flag set before the macrotask await (checked by `send()`, refusing honestly like the existing queue-full case) would close the window. Not a correctness bug — the flush still happens after — just a narrower race than the design intends.

- [ ] `SESSION_CREATE` native-resume split-refusal branch has no direct test `bug` `#tests` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-23)
  Found in M2 final review: the native resume path's split-refusal branch (the three distinct cwd-resolution refusal messages, see `docs/conversations.md` → "Native provider participation") has no IPC-harness test exercising it directly — it existed before M2 and M2 didn't add coverage. `session-meta-parity.test.ts` (M2, real-store IPC harness) now makes a direct test possible where none existed before; write one as a follow-up rather than relying on incidental coverage.

- [ ] `sync-spaces-engine.test.ts` "debounces file changes into one sync" — **recurring macOS CI failure. Possibly a real sync watcher bug, not a test defect** `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-07-22 as a flake; ESCALATED 2026-07-23; **DE-ESCALATED 2026-08-12 — "macOS CI is RED for every PR" is no longer true: only 22 of the last 60 Desktop CI master runs failed, the debounce case passed in the latest red run, and it last failed 2026-08-06 (run 31079675247). Still real, still recurring, still unexplained.** Separately: the macOS reds of 2026-08-10/11 — runs 31526973244, 31531850229, 31539413917 — are a DIFFERENT test, `sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts > skips the file write when nothing changed`, which deserves its own entry, added below.)
  Seen once on youcoded PR #213 (run 29983190137, macOS, commit `5850e281` — a branch that touches nothing in sync-spaces): `vi.waitFor(() => expect(t.pushes.length).toBe(1))` timed out with 0 pushes. Same macOS fs-event/timing flake family as the closed `subagent-watcher` and `sync-warnings-lifecycle` entries — and the SAME FILE as the now-CLOSED `[x]` "sync-spaces-engine macOS CI flake — fs.watch test still blows the 5s vitest timeout" entry further down (ROADMAP :326, PR #180/#181 history), which prescribed a per-test `testTimeout` — that fix shipped and closed the entry, but did NOT cure this. Treat these as one ledger, and note that entry called the failure mode exactly: *"trains people to re-run red macOS builds without reading them — which is how a real failure gets merged."* Passed on rerun (job 89132331505). **RECURRED 2026-07-23** on the same PR's final head `bebbe276` (job 89313264909, macOS, 60030ms timeout on the same `debounces file changes into one sync` case) — again on a branch touching nothing in sync-spaces, and again green on rerun. Per the flake rule (file once, fix on recurrence) this is now due. **RECURRED A THIRD TIME 2026-07-23** on youcoded PR #243 (job 89320861447, a COMMENT-ONLY diff), which is what finally pinned the diagnosis:

  **The prescribed timeout fix is already applied and is NOT the answer — do not re-apply it.** `sync-spaces-engine.test.ts` already carries both knobs (`WAIT_MS = 60_000` bounding the `vi.waitFor` poll, and `vi.setConfig({ testTimeout: 120_000 })` bounding the test, added in the #180/#181 round). The failing run polls the full 60s and observes **0 pushes** — not a slow push, no push at all. A budget that is already 60s/120s cannot be "too tight"; the watcher event is never delivered, so raising any timeout can never fix it.

  **ESCALATED 2026-07-23 — no longer intermittent.** On #243 it failed on the first run AND on the rerun (jobs 89320861447, 89323296359), both at the identical ~2m30s / 60s-poll signature, 293 test files passing and only this one failing. Every prior occurrence went green on rerun; this one does not. Treat macOS CI as effectively red for every PR until it is fixed — #243 was merged over it deliberately (comment-only diff, and the same failure already exists on master via `bebbe276`), but that is not a repeatable escape hatch.

  **PARTIALLY WALKED BACK 2026-07-25 — it went green on rerun again, so "no longer intermittent" is too strong.** Observed on `origin/master` at `95959cff` (Desktop CI run 30154294363): macOS failed the same `debounces file changes into one sync` case with `AssertionError: expected +0 to be 1`, Windows and Ubuntu green (303 passed / 1 failed) — then `gh run rerun --failed` came back **success** with no code change. It also failed on master at `60d56a67` on 2026-07-23 (run 30048860023), so it does reproduce on master, not just on PR branches. Two corrections to the escalation above: the failure is **not** deterministic (this rerun disproves "every rerun stays red"), and the fast-fail signature differs from the one the diagnosis was pinned on — this run asserted `+0 to be 1` quickly rather than polling the full 60s, so it is not obviously the same 0-pushes-after-60s shape. Everything else in this entry stands: it is real, it recurs on branches that touch nothing in sync-spaces, and the timeout knobs are not the answer. Treat the intermittency as evidence about the mechanism (a race that usually loses under load), not as a reason to downgrade it.

  **This may be a REAL macOS bug, not a test defect — do not "fix" it by weakening the test.** If the hypothesis below holds, files written immediately after a space is added are silently missed in production on macOS too, which is a data-loss-shaped bug in sync, not a CI annoyance. Confirm the production path before touching the test; a test tweak that makes this green while `addSpace` still resolves early would hide the real defect.

  Real hypothesis to chase: a **watcher-arming race**. The test calls `await engine.addSpace(space)` and then immediately `writeFileSync`s two files. On macOS, FSEvents registration is not synchronous with the `addSpace` promise resolving, so writes landing in that window are missed entirely and no debounce ever fires — which matches "0 pushes, forever." ~~Fix direction is to make `addSpace` resolve only once the watcher is genuinely armed~~ **THE PRESCRIPTION IS ALREADY THE CODE (checked 2026-08-12): `engine.ts:105-108` has awaited `watcher.once('ready')` since `c898c046` (2026-07-08) — i.e. since BEFORE this entry was filed. So chokidar's `ready` is evidently not the same as FSEvents being genuinely armed, and the next investigation needs a new hypothesis (e.g. a post-`ready` FSEvents settling window), NOT this one re-applied.** Cost so far: a re-run lap on three separate heads.

- [ ] `sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts` "skips the file write when nothing changed" fails repeatedly on macOS CI `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-08-12, found while re-verifying the debounce entry above)
  Three consecutive macOS reds on master 2026-08-10/11 (Desktop CI runs 31526973244, 31531850229, 31539413917) are all this one case — `runHealthCheck — resolved warnings clear themselves > skips the file write when nothing changed` — with 404 other files passing. The test guards the PR #292 "rewrite only when the set actually changes" behavior. Ubuntu/Windows green, macOS-only, same fs/timing-flake family as the rest of this ledger. Not yet root-caused; note it postdates PR #292 (merged 2026-08-11), so check first whether the test's own clock/fs assumptions are macOS-hostile before suspecting the product code.

- [x] `session-meta-native-refusal.test.ts` flaked locally under parallel workers (`/tmp` race) `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-23, **MOOT — the file was deleted 2026-07-22 in youcoded `c3db2742`, one day before this entry was filed; its successor `session-meta-parity.test.ts` already uses per-run `mkdtempSync` dirs, the prescribed fix. Verified 2026-08-12.**)
