- [x] `--destructive` is over-constrained — one token serves two opposed jobs `bug` `#themes` `#a11y` (added 2026-07-22; FIXED same day in youcoded #197 (merged `694fc94b`) + wecoded-marketplace #51 (merged `facf695`) — token split into `--destructive` (fill) and `--destructive-fg` (text), derived per theme by `deriveDestructiveFg()`; all 10 `text-destructive` sites migrated; two new HARD audit rules cover destructive-as-text, which had NO rule before. Derived: midnight/dark `#D45C5C`, halftone `#D15353`, light `#C62828` unchanged, creme `#BE2626`)
  The token is used BOTH as a filled surface (`bg-destructive` on danger buttons/toggles, where the label needs contrast *against* it) AND as text (`text-destructive`, ~10 sites: skip-permissions warnings at `text-[10px]`, `states.tsx:164` at `text-3xs`, `danger-outline` labels, error strings — where it needs contrast *against the canvas*). Those pull in opposite directions on dark themes: a fill wants to be DARK so a white label reads; text on a dark canvas wants to be LIGHT. **Proved by sweep: ZERO reds satisfy both roles at AA (4.5) across all five themes.** At AA-large (3.0) for the text role, 495 do — but most `text-destructive` sites render at 10-12px, where AA-large does not apply. So no choice of hex fixes this; only splitting the token does. Measured for `#C62828` (text on canvas/panel): midnight 3.37/3.08, dark 3.36/3.13, halftone 3.58/3.39, light 5.02/4.67, creme 4.88/4.34 — i.e. the darkening that fixed the danger-button label (:141) moved the failure onto dark-theme warning text, where the old `#DD4444` had been passing (4.49-4.78). Not a regression *introduced* by :141 so much as the same over-constraint surfacing at the other end. Fix is the `--accent`/`--on-accent` pattern applied here: a fill token plus a separate text token derived per-theme (light themes darken it, dark themes lighten it). Note the contrast audit has NO rule for `destructive on canvas/panel` — adding one today would fail, which is why this is filed rather than bolted onto #197.
  `#DD4444` scores 4.213:1 against white and 4.131:1 against near-black — both under the 4.5 WCAG AA bar for small text, and danger button labels render at `text-xs` (12px) / `text-2xs` (11px). So every danger button in every theme ships a sub-AA label today, and no choice of label color fixes it; only darkening the red does (`#D33A3A` → 4.73:1, `#C62828` → 5.62:1, `#C0392B` → 5.44:1). Surfaced by the UI-consistency change-43 work (youcoded `4424c7d8`), which added an `on-destructive/destructive` pair to the contrast audit — that pair currently prints ✗ for all 11 themes (4 built-in + 7 community), since none override `overlay.destructive` and they all inherit the default. **Not caused by change 43** — it made a long-standing failure measurable. Note the spec's change-43 text asserts "#DD4444 vs white = ~4.7:1"; that figure is wrong (it matches `#D33A3A`), and the "white if ≥4.5, else near-black" rule it justified would have flipped every danger button to near-black at LOWER contrast — the engine ships a max-contrast pick instead (pinned in `tests/theme-engine.test.ts`). Decide: darken the default red (visible change to every danger surface across all themes — wants a render), or relax that one audit pair to 3.0 (AA-large) and accept the status quo. Audit is advisory-only (not wired to CI, despite the spec's §6 calling it a "CI contrast audit"), so nothing is blocked meanwhile.

- [ ] Five latent bugs surfaced by the tranche-2 input migration (none introduced by it) `bug` `#ui` (added 2026-07-19; **BUGS 2, 3, 4 FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #297, merge `3d14ca6e`** (invisible focus state → `border-accent`; PartialRow.resume silent no-op → sibling-style inline errors incl. the previously-unhandled download() rejection; RatingSubmitModal fabricated 'Network error' → handleSubmit-parity offline distinction). All red-first-tested. **Destin: the focus-state change is visual — eyeball the search pill on its THREE surfaces: Session Files drawer, Project View files tab, and the Model Picker search field.** Bugs 1 (label nesting) and 5 (particles `as any`) remain open below. **New findings from the #297 review, same family:** (a) the TypeError-offline branch in BOTH RatingSubmitModal handlers is likely unreachable on desktop — `wrap()` (`handler-utils.ts:16`) converts network failures into `{ok:false, status:0}` results, so a real offline install surfaces as the fabricated `'Install this plugin first to rate it.'`; (b) Electron's `Error invoking remote method '…':` prefix leaks into all model-row inline errors app-wide. Both belong to the v1.3.1 misleading-errors audit.)
  All five were found by reading code at the edit site during youcoded PR #183 and deliberately NOT fixed there — each is a behavior change that doesn't belong in a control migration. Full context in spec §13.7. (1) **Sync wizard label nesting** — `SyncSetupWizard`'s two repo text inputs are nested INSIDE the radio's `<label>`; label activation forwards to the first labelable descendant (the radio), so clicking into the text field can re-trigger it. Harmless today only because the field renders only when that radio is already checked; would bite the moment those fields show for the unselected option. Fixing means restructuring the label. (2) **The search pill has an invisible focus state — now at `ui/SearchFilterPill.tsx:72`** (the pill was extracted from `ProjectView.tsx:644` into a shared component and the defect rode along verbatim; re-verified 2026-08-12) — `focus-within:border-edge-dim` over a resting `border-edge`, i.e. focus has LESS contrast than resting. Reads like it was meant to be `border-accent`. Real a11y gap. (3) **`LocalModelsSection.PartialRow.resume()` silently no-ops** — the inner catch swallows the `quants()` failure and `if (opt)` then skips the download, so clicking Resume on a partial from a prior session while Hugging Face is unreachable does nothing at all: no error, no state change. Every sibling in that file (`RepoCard`, `QuantDownloadRow`, `InstalledRow`, `OtherLocalApps`) has an explicit error line; `resume` is the one that doesn't. (4) **`RatingSubmitModal.handleInstallAndRate` fabricates a cause** — discards the caught `err` and reports a hardcoded `'Network error — try again.'`, the exact pattern `docs/error-message-standards.md` forbids; `handleSubmit` directly above it does it correctly (distinguishes `TypeError`/offline from other failures). This one is a direct instance of the standing "never write misleading error messages" rule. (5) **`ThemeScreen` particles is cast `as any`** so a theme JSON can carry an arbitrary string — the native `<select>` rendered an out-of-list value blank, and `Select` now shows the "Select…" placeholder, meaning a theme with e.g. `particles: "sparkle"` displays as unset and gets silently overwritten on the next change. Pre-existing, just surfaced differently.

- [x] Contrast audit only checks `canvas`, so secondary text goes invisible on raised surfaces `bug` `#themes` `#a11y` (added 2026-07-19, **shipped 2026-07-20**) — wecoded-marketplace#50 (OKLCH solver + 34-rule generated table + `--fix` authoring flow), wecoded-themes#19 (7 repalettes, `::selection` fix, vendored rules, CI blocking + drift guard), youcoded#187 (4 built-ins, `globals.css` lockstep, third-copy consolidation, 106-site `fg-faint`→`fg-muted` migration). All 11 themes now pass HARD+SURFACE glass-composited. Analysis archived under `docs/archive/investigations/` and `docs/archive/prototypes/`.
  Repro (Destin, 2026-07-19, Meadow Mist): the second line of the "Compacting conversation…" card is effectively invisible — screenshot shows pale green on pale green. Cause is the ruleset, not the theme. `CompactingCard.tsx:27` is `text-xs text-fg-muted` on a `bg-inset` bubble; Meadow Mist has `fg-muted #7C9384` on `inset #BAD0B6` = **2.01:1**. It passes CI because `wecoded-themes/scripts/audit-contrast.mjs` measures all five fg tokens against **`canvas` only** (`fg-muted/canvas` = 3.13, just over its 3.0 gate) and spot-checks `inset` with a single pair (`fg/inset`). `canvas` is the *lightest* surface in a light theme, so auditing against it alone measures the best case and ships the worst — full Meadow Mist matrix: on panel `fg-muted`=2.63 `fg-faint`=1.62; on inset 2.01 / 1.24; on well 2.25 / 1.39. Three notes: (1) not one card — `text-fg-muted`/`text-fg-faint` on a non-canvas surface is a common renderer pairing, this is just where it got visible; (2) glass makes it worse and the audit can't see it — Meadow Mist is a wallpaper theme at `panels-opacity: 0.58`, so the real backdrop is `inset` mixed toward a photo and the true ratio is below the flat-token 2.01; (3) any widened matrix will fail already-published themes, so measure the existing set BEFORE picking gates or the rule gets written to whatever happens to pass. Two directions, not exclusive: widen the matrix (every fg token × every surface it can land on, with a grandfather list or version ramp), or constrain the pairing instead (`fg-muted`/`fg-faint` become canvas-only tokens; secondary text on a raised surface must be `fg-dim` or better — cheaper for theme authors, needs a guard or it decays). Patching `CompactingCard` to `text-fg-dim` is a one-line stopgap but hides the class of bug — land it WITH the rule change, not instead of it. **Second report same day (Settings → Appearance row descriptions invisible) made this much bigger — see spec §12.3.** That one is `SettingsRow.tsx:34` (`text-[10px] text-fg-muted` on the `bg-inset/50` row at `:28`): one line, **18 call sites**. A renderer sweep then showed most of the app isn't on canvas at all — `.layer-surface` (`globals.css:811`) paints `--panel` under **55 `<OverlayPanel>` usages across 45 files**, `.settings-drawer` (`:648`) under all of Settings, and `AssistantTurnBubble.tsx:362` paints `bg-inset` under every assistant message and ToolCard. Measured across all 11 shipped themes (4 built-in + 7 community): `fg-muted`/`inset` is under 3.0 in **9 of 11**; `fg-faint` on `panel` or `inset` is under 2.4 in **11 of 11** (best 2.31 Strawberry Kitty, worst 1.24 Meadow Mist). **`fg-faint` on a raised surface has never worked in any theme that has ever shipped** — an app pairing no theme can satisfy, since its 1.8-vs-canvas gate assumed decorative use. It isn't always decorative: `StatusBar.tsx:756–1017` has ~15 `bg-panel` pills whose entire content is 10px `text-fg-faint` (`In:`/`Out:`/`Cached:`/`Speed:`, and at `:914`/`:863` the strings "No changes" and "N/A"); `ThemeScreen.tsx:377/433/481/486` puts `text-fg-faint` on the SAME element as `bg-inset`; `SettingsPanel.tsx:1301` runs 9px faint permission descriptions. On the muted tier: `UsageCard.tsx:85–166` (every cost/token label), `ResumeBrowser.tsx:463–549` (all field labels), `SkillCard.tsx:141/205`, `ToolBody.tsx` (~54 occurrences). `ui/states.tsx:119` is the same bug in a shared primitive with zero call sites — latent, fix before adoption. **This kills the widen-the-matrix option** (a gate that fails 11/11 themes isn't a rule) and favours constraining the pairing: make `fg-muted`/`fg-faint` canvas-only, require `fg-dim`+ for substantive text on raised surfaces. Cheap mechanism to evaluate: `.layer-surface` already precedents descendant overrides at `globals.css:827` (the protection cascade), so `.layer-surface .text-fg-muted { color: var(--fg-dim) }` could cover all 55 overlay sites with no component churn — UNVERIFIED, must respect the same layered-vs-unlayered ordering trap, and needs sibling rules for `.settings-drawer` + the bubble. Two things it wouldn't solve: genuinely decorative faint text (separators, timestamps, placeholders) gets lifted too unless the token splits, and glass still isn't modelled (all figures are flat-token; wallpaper themes composite toward a photo and are worse). Fix order: `SettingsRow.tsx:34` → `ui/states.tsx:119` → the descendant rule → StatusBar's faint pills. Full analysis + both contrast matrices: **spec §12** (`docs/archive/specs/2026-07-16-ui-consistency-design-spec.md`). Split across repos: app owns the pairing fix (+ a `react-renderer` rule), wecoded-themes owns the audit. Not part of the UI-consistency tranches; shouldn't block them.

- [x] Linux download page still hands everyone the AppImage (FUSE step) once v1.3 ships native packages `bug` `#docs` `#install` (added 2026-07-16, shipped 2026-07-16)
  `youcoded/docs/index.html` matchers hardcode `'dl-linux': /\.AppImage$/`, so when v1.3 ships the deb/rpm/pacman targets added in youcoded#98 (2026-05-20 — postdates the v1.2.4 tag, which is why v1.2.4 offers only the AppImage), every Linux user is still handed the AppImage and its `sudo apt install libfuse2` step. **Verified in a clean Ubuntu 24.04 VM 2026-07-16:** the shipped AppImage dies with `dlopen(): error loading libfuse.so.2` on stock 24.04 (no libfuse2), while the locally-built `.deb` declares **zero** fuse deps, `apt install ./youcoded_*.deb` resolves everything itself, installs to `/opt/YouCoded`, and registers a real menu entry the AppImage never provides. Fix: offer deb → Debian/Ubuntu/Mint, rpm → Fedora, pacman → Arch, AppImage as any-distro fallback; keep the FUSE note only on the AppImage path (its `sudo apt install libfuse2` text is CORRECT — verified: libfuse2 no longer exists on 24.04 but apt resolves it to libfuse2t64 via Provides; don't "fix" it). Also un-blocks testing those three artifacts, which no release has ever shipped. See docs/vm-testing.md. **SHIPPED 2026-07-16 — youcoded PR #153 (merge `e2285d54`), live on the Pages site (verified by fetching the deployed URL).** The Linux modal now asks the distro once and hands over deb/rpm/pacman, with the FUSE note scoped to the AppImage variant alone. Options are built from the assets each release actually carries, so today's page is unchanged (v1.2.4 has no native packages) and the picker lights up on its own when v1.3 ships them — no follow-up change needed.

The colour/token half is deleted and replaced with a pointer table to the files that are the real spec; the mascot-rig + scene-companion sections are KEPT — re-verified 2026-07-22, every file and type they cite still exists and that content lives nowhere else in this repo. Did not wait on the UI-consistency spec §8 token reference, which is still unapproved and unwritten)
  Still says "DestinCode", names the wrong localStorage key (`destincode-theme`; actual `youcoded-theme`), and instructs the pre-engine workflow ([data-theme] CSS blocks + ThemeName union + DARK_THEMES array) that no longer matches the JSON + theme-engine system. Rewrite against theme-engine.ts/theme-context.tsx — or retire it into the ui-primitives/token reference doc proposed in the UI-consistency spec §8. Found during the 2026-07-16 token audit.

- [x] Ink prompt title/description hijacked by "trust" substring collision `bug` `#renderer` `#terminal-parser` (added 2026-07-16; fixed 2026-07-16, youcoded PR #157 — trust anchor re-keyed to the security-note phrase 'files you trust', TrustGate exact-matches TRUST_PROMPT_TITLE, safeguard prompt got its own 'Message Flagged' anchor + SETUP entry; 'dark mode'/'login method' keys tightened the same way; Android parser mirrored incl. nearby-lines scan window)
  Repro: Fable 5 model-switch safeguard prompt renders with the "Trust This Folder?" title and hardcoded "Claude needs your permission before working in this directory." body, but the real buttons ("Switch to Opus 4.8" / "Edit prompt and retry with Fable 5"). Root cause is two independent bare substring matches on the word "trust": (1) `ink-select-parser.ts` `TITLE_OVERRIDES['trust']` — `extractTitle()` scans the raw terminal text above ANY Ink menu for the substring `"trust"` and force-labels it `'Trust This Folder?'`; the Fable safeguard prompt's body text apparently contains "trust" somewhere, so it collides. (2) `TrustGate.tsx` `findTrustPrompt()` independently does `title.includes('trust')`, so once (1) mislabels the title, TrustGate takes over rendering and swaps in its own static copy while keeping the real (correctly-parsed) buttons array. Fix needs a non-substring anchor for both matches (e.g. an exact-title check, or a more specific keyword phrase unlikely to appear in other prompts' body text) — any future Ink prompt whose text happens to contain "trust" will hit this same bug. Screenshot on file from Destin, 2026-07-16.
- [x] Stale "answer the prompt first" toast permanently blocks chat send `bug` `#renderer` `#terminal-parser` (added 2026-07-16; #157 was a PARTIAL fix — real recurring cause found + FIXED 2026-07-17, youcoded PR #165 merge `49bcf49b`: exclude the `_history_expand` marker from the gate (shared `HISTORY_EXPAND_PROMPT_ID` const) + re-check the menu is still on screen before the detector's show-timer fires. Pinned by pty-input-gate.test.ts + use-prompt-detector.test.tsx. See correction at end for the full diagnosis + the three lower-severity cousins still open. Original #157 note: fixed 2026-07-16, youcoded PR #157 — on menu-id change usePromptDetector now cancels the pending show timer and dismisses the previously SHOWN prompt before the SETUP_PROMPT_TITLES gate can bail; gated on shownPromptRef so streaming-list id churn dispatches nothing. The "Send Anyway" override affordance moved to its own Features entry below)
  Repro: resume one or more sessions (ResumeBrowser → `session-manager.ts` launches `claude --resume <id>`, which shows CC's own "Resuming from a summary" Ink prompt — `SETUP_PROMPT_TITLES` has 'Resume Session'). If the underlying PTY screen advances to any OTHER Ink-parseable content (a permission Yes/No/Always-Allow menu, or even a false-positive numbered list in Claude's plain-text output) before the user clicks the Resume Session card, the card gets permanently stuck with `completed: false` and `InputBar.tsx`/`App.tsx` refuse all future sends with "Claude is waiting for your response — answer the prompt first," even though nothing is live on screen. Destin hit this on multiple sessions resumed after a crash — consistent with the mechanism, since every resumed session independently replays the same Resume-Session-card → next-PTY-state sequence. Root cause: `usePromptDetector.ts` lines 117-123 — `lastMenuRef.current.set(sid, menu.id)` runs BEFORE the `SETUP_PROMPT_TITLES` check, so when a new (unrecognized) menu is detected, the ref is overwritten to point at it and the function returns early without dismissing the OLD prompt. Dismissal (`DISMISS_PROMPT`, the `else if (lastMenuId)` branch) only ever fires for whatever id `lastMenuRef` *currently* holds, so the orphaned old entry can never be cleared by the parser again — only a direct button click (`COMPLETE_PROMPT`) would have cleared it, and that didn't happen. Fix: don't clobber `lastMenuRef` for unrecognized menus (only overwrite it when the new menu is itself a recognized `SETUP_PROMPT_TITLES` entry, or explicitly dismiss the old promptId before switching to track a new one). Destin also wants a "Send Anyway" override affordance on the blocking toast/gate as a user-facing safety valve regardless of root cause — a human who can see there's no live prompt should be able to force the send rather than being permanently locked out of chat.
  **CORRECTION 2026-07-17 — #157 fixed a real but MINOR path; the recurring bug Destin keeps hitting is a different, DETERMINISTIC cause #157 never touched.** Reproduced live on a resumed session. Root cause: `hasPendingInteraction` (`pty-input-gate.ts`) blocks on any `kind:'prompt'` timeline entry that isn't `completed`, and on resume `App.tsx` dispatches `HISTORY_LOADED` with `hasMore:true` for any session with history, which pushes a `_history_expand` "See previous messages" entry — no buttons, no `completed` — so `!undefined===true` trips the gate on EVERY resumed session until the user clicks "See previous messages." `ChatView` already special-cased that id when rendering; the gate never learned the exception. This is why #157 (an Ink-menu detector fix) couldn't help — `_history_expand` is a UI affordance from a different code path. Fix in **youcoded PR #165** (pending merge): exclude the marker id in the gate via a shared `HISTORY_EXPAND_PROMPT_ID` constant, + a second fix for a timing race where `usePromptDetector`'s show-timer fired `SHOW_PROMPT` for a menu that vanished during the 350ms debounce (re-check the screen before showing). Both pinned by tests. Systemic note: the two limbs of `hasPendingInteraction` clear asymmetrically — the awaiting-approval limb self-heals via `endTurn()` on every turn end, the prompt-entry limb is NEVER touched by `endTurn`, so a stranded prompt entry persists until its one dedicated clearer fires. **Lower-severity cousins found in the same investigation, NOT fixed by #165 (own follow-up entries needed):** (a) a remote client hydrating a `completed:false` prompt strands it (its detector refs are empty and `DISMISS_PROMPT` isn't relayed cross-device); (b) Android native `promptShow` with no matching dismiss/complete; (c) buddy `BubbleFeed` prompt-card click writes to the PTY but never dispatches `COMPLETE_PROMPT`, so a stuck prompt can't be cleared from the buddy view at all. (These three carry into the follow-up entry below.)

- [ ] Pending-prompt clear gaps not covered by #165 (three cousins) `bug` `#renderer` `#android` `#remote` (added 2026-07-17)
  Surfaced in the #165 investigation but out of that PR's scope; all three are variants of the same `hasPendingInteraction` limb-(b) "a prompt entry can strand at `completed:false` with no path to clear it" family. (a) **Remote-hydrate strand** — `serializeChatState` ships the full timeline incl. uncompleted prompt entries; a freshly-connected remote client has empty `usePromptDetector` refs and `DISMISS_PROMPT` isn't relayed cross-device, so a hydrated prompt not reproduced in the remote's replayed buffer strands there. (b) **Android native `promptShow`** (`App.tsx` ~1201) dispatches SHOW_PROMPT from the native bridge with no renderer-side fallback clear — a missed `promptDismiss`/`promptComplete` poll tick strands it. (c) **Buddy `BubbleFeed`** (`BubbleFeed.tsx` ~360) prompt-card click writes to the PTY but never dispatches `COMPLETE_PROMPT`, so a stuck prompt can't be cleared from the buddy view at all (desktop `ChatView.handlePromptSelect` does both). Systemic option worth weighing: give the marker/affordance prompts a distinct non-`prompt` timeline `kind` (or have `endTurn()` also drop uncompleted prompt entries — but NOT naively; a genuinely-live Ink menu after an interrupt would lose its card while the PTY still owns the keystroke). The #165 "Send anyway" ESC-first valve already gives users a manual escape hatch for all three regardless.
- [x] Spurious "session taken over on another device" on an idle, untouched session `bug` `#sync` `#leases` (added 2026-07-16; fixed 2026-07-16, youcoded PR #157 + wecoded-marketplace PR #43 deployed — client renewTick distinguishes holder-present (genuine takeover, now attributed with the device name) from holder-null (lapse → silent re-acquire); DO renew against a free lease revives it so old clients heal server-side)

- [x] Terminal view Ctrl+V pastes twice `bug` `#renderer` `#terminal` (added + fixed 2026-07-16, youcoded PR #157)
  Reported by Destin 2026-07-16. TerminalView's custom Ctrl+V handler called terminal.paste() and returned false, but returning false from attachCustomKeyEventHandler doesn't preventDefault — Chromium still fired its native paste event into xterm's hidden textarea, whose own paste listener wrote the clipboard to the PTY a second time. Fix: preventDefault the keydown so the custom path (which must exist for macOS, where Electron apps without an Edit menu role have no native paste shortcut) is the only path. Handler extracted to terminal-key-handler.ts with pinning tests. Confirm with one Ctrl+V in the next dev/build session.

- [ ] Android ManagedSession bare-phrase screen scans can false-fire prompt cards `bug` `#android` `#terminal-parser` (added 2026-07-16)
  Same class as the fixed "trust" substring collision, found in the 2026-07-16 sweep: ManagedSession.kt's non-menu prompt heuristics match bare phrases against the whole lowercased screen — the paste-code card fires on ("paste code"|"paste the code"|"browser") + ("sign"|"code"|"authorize"), and the Continue/"Ready" card fires on "press enter to continue" — so Claude conversation output containing those phrases (e.g. instructions that literally say "press Enter to continue") creates phantom prompt cards on Android. Not fixed in the sweep because these gate the Android sign-in flow and need on-device verification; fix should anchor on option shape / distinctive Ink chrome rather than conversation-plausible phrases.
  Repro: Destin sees the MovedGate ("This session was taken over on another device") after just opening a fresh session and leaving it idle / browsing other menus — no other device involved. Root cause spans both sides of the lease heartbeat: (1) client `lease-client.ts` `renewTick` (lines 142-165) renews every 30s via an `unref()`'d `setTimeout`, and on ANY `!r.ok` renew response unconditionally treats it as "another device force-acquired it," tearing the session down and calling `opts.onTakeoverRequest(sessionId)` with **no `from`** — that missing `from` is exactly why `MovedGate.tsx:39` falls back to the generic "another device" text (a genuine cross-device takeover always carries the real device name). (2) server `wecoded-marketplace/worker/src/sync/room.ts` lazily expires any lease record older than the 300s TTL on the NEXT op (`if (rec && rec.expiresAt <= now) rec = null`, line ~168), and `renew` only succeeds `if (rec && rec.deviceId === deviceId)` (line 183) — so `!r.ok` on renew has two conflated causes: genuinely stolen, OR nobody stole it, our own heartbeat just missed its 300s (ten 30s beats) window. A `setTimeout` heartbeat is suspended by system sleep/lock or OS throttling of an idle/backgrounded process — any such gap misreports as a hostile takeover of a session nothing else ever touched. Fix: on renew failure, `query()` (op `get`) the actual current holder before tearing down — only surface MovedGate if held by a genuinely different `deviceId`; if unheld, silently re-acquire and continue. Confirmed against the DO's own lazy-expiry test (`wecoded-marketplace/worker/test/sync-hub.test.ts` ~L279-289).

- [x] StatusBar model chip always reads "Model Unknown" for native/OpenRouter sessions `bug` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-18, **SHIPPED 2026-07-19 — youcoded PR #185, merge `9e7de571`**)
  **Shipped as diagnosed, with one correction and one adjacent bug folded in.** Correction: the fix does NOT go through `useActiveSessionModel` as this entry proposed — that hook reads `turn.model` off timeline entries, so it's empty until the first assistant turn lands. `SessionInfo.model` is correct the moment the session exists, is kept live on every swap by `onNativeModelChanged` (`App.tsx:3039`), and was ALREADY reaching the renderer for the picker's current-selection highlight (`App.tsx:3036`) — so the fix needed zero new plumbing. **Adjacent bug found while tracing and fixed in the same PR (it was worse than the chip):** `cycleModel` (Shift+Space) had no provider gate. `SessionManager.sendInput` returns `false` for a worker-less native session, but `guardedPtySend` DISCARDS that return value, so the `if (!guardedPtySend(...)) return;` bail never fired and execution reached the optimistic writes — relabeling the chip with a CC alias the session is not running (`indexOf('unknown')` → -1 → wraps to `haiku`) AND writing that alias to the GLOBAL model preference. A confidently wrong chip outranks an honest error chip. Design calls worth not re-deriving: `ModelChip` is a display-only union rather than a widened `ModelAlias` (which also drives `cycleModel`, the `canAuto` gate at `App.tsx:2394`, and the persisted preference — a third-party model id must not leak into any of them); the label is a local pure prettifier, NOT the picker's catalog labels, because `ModelCatalog` is network-backed behind a TTL'd cache, async, empty without provider keys, and has NO rows for `openai-compatible` custom endpoints (`model-catalog.ts:187`) — the raw id always rides in the chip's `title`; chip color is `var(--tag-blue)` via TagChip's `color-mix` formula, the one tag slot colliding with nothing else in the bar (teal is Haiku, indigo is Opus, gray is Sonnet, fuchsia is Fable, red is both Unknown chips, accent/amber/salmon are permission modes). Pinned by `tests/native-model-label.test.ts` + `tests/model-chip.test.ts` (17 tests). **Not yet eyeballed in a dev instance** — the blue against each theme and truncation on a long GGUF name are queued visual checks.

- [x] `harness-stall-watchdog` flakes on Windows CI — a 15ms real-time stall budget `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-19)
  **SHIPPED 2026-07-22 — youcoded PR #198 (merge `79ac5802`), confirmed exactly as diagnosed.** Mechanism, now measured: Windows' default timer resolution is ~15.6ms, so `setTimeout(15)` fires on the first tick AT OR PAST the budget — the warning timer and the retry's first chunk landed in the same tick and scheduler noise picked the winner, which is why it was Windows-only and green on a plain re-run. Headroom was thin even on an idle 32-core Linux box: sweeping the budget, a spurious second warning first appears at 10ms (1/60) and is the majority outcome by 3ms (53/60), so 15ms was only ~4x the ideal path. Took the roadmap's second option (generous budget, not fake timers) because the file drives live stream reads on real timers by design: `STALL_MS = 250` (~16x the Windows tick) plus `STREAMING_WINDOW_MS = 400` for the keeps-emitting case, whose margin runs the OTHER way — its 4ms chunk spacing must stay far under the window or a hiccup fires a watchdog that case asserts never fires. Note the follow-on trap this created: at 250ms the both-attempts-stall case burns ~1.0s of real time against vitest's DEFAULT 5000ms `testTimeout`, dropping headroom from ~300x to ~5x — so the file now carries an explicit `vi.setConfig` too. Raising a budget can just move the bet up one level.
  `tests/harness-stall-watchdog.test.ts > "silent stall with nothing streamed: warns (willRetry) then AUTO-RETRIES and completes"` failed Windows-only on youcoded PR #185 with `AssertionError: expected [...] to have a length of 1 but got 2`, then passed on a plain re-run with no code change (and 10/10 in isolation on Linux). The test's `makeOpts` sets `stallWarningMs: 15, stallCountdownMs: 15` — a **15 millisecond** real-time budget. The case asserts exactly ONE stall warning: attempt 0 stalls, the retry succeeds. Under Windows parallel load the RETRY's own `completingStream` doesn't deliver its first chunk within 15ms either, so the watchdog fires a second warning and the length assertion fails. **Same fixed-budget family as the `subagent-watcher` and git-heavy-sync entries above, and the same fix shape** — the budget is the bug, not the symptom: either drive this file on fake timers (it asserts on ordering/count, not on wall-clock, so it has no reason to race a real clock at all) or give the real-time budgets enough headroom that runner load can't close the gap. Note the file was NOT touched by #185 (renderer-only diff) and tests `src/main/harness/harness-session.ts`. Costs a CI re-run each time it fires, and trains people to re-run red Windows builds without reading them. (found 2026-07-19 merging youcoded#185)

- [ ] "Jump to bottom" overlay button collides with other menu layers `bug` `#renderer` `#ui` (added 2026-07-22, **PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-07-28 — youcoded `7cecca1a` (PR #260, merge `4e51a934`)**)
  Repro (Destin, 2026-07-22): the floating "Jump to bottom" button that appears when scrolled up overlapped other floating UI like the "Model unloaded" notice or permission prompts. **The reported repro is fixed**: `.jump-to-bottom` now offsets by a measured `--model-status-height` (plus `--queued-strip-height`), so it no longer shares the model-status strip's byte-identical offset. **What remains is the second half:** the stacking-order audit of ALL ChatView floats (attention classifier toast, permission gates, model-loading spinners, sync status) — `globals.css:571` notes the two strips coordinate by *offset, not z-index*, so nothing about the fix generalizes to the other floats.

- [x] Device `lastSeen` is a launch stamp, not a heartbeat `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-16; FIXED 2026-07-18)
  `upsertSelf` runs once at `main.ts:1573`; nothing bumps `lastSeen` while the app runs, so "last seen 4 hours ago" actually means "last launched 4 hours ago". **Fixed via per-device sync recency carried over the SyncHub DO — deliberately NOT the git heartbeat this note worried about (no file-write-per-bump, no history bloat).** The `SyncGroupRoom` DO now stores a durable per-account `lastSyncByDevice` map (keyed by machineId), fed by the existing `pushed:true` signal (which now also carries `deviceId` + server `at`) and shipped in `hello`; device rows show "Synced just now" (<5min) / "Last synced X ago" via the pure `deviceActivityLabel`; self reads the local live `lastSyncEpoch` (no longer hard-codes "active now"). Backward-compatible rollout. Ships: wecoded-marketplace PR #45, youcoded PR #171 + #170. Spec+plan: `docs/archive/{specs,plans}/2026-07-17-sync-menu-recency-*.md`.

- [x] Workspace CI is red on 8 store-budget overruns `bug` `#docs` `#tooling` (added 2026-07-30, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — same work as the fuller "youcoded-dev's OWN CI is red" entry above (youcoded PR #302 + workspace rule trims): audit-anchors now exits 0.**)
  `.github/workflows/workspace-ci.yml` (added 2026-07-30, merge `b447c8e`) runs `audit-anchors.mjs` on every push and on a daily cron; it exits 1 today, with two distinct failure classes. ~~**Self-clearing:** 2 stale `verify:` anchors in `.claude/rules/react-renderer.md` plus 4 missing `docs/MAP.md` paths, all pointing at `desktop/src/renderer/dev/workbench/`.~~ **CLEARED 2026-07-30** when `feat/ui-workbench` merged (youcoded PR #273 `9fa50b14`): `anchors 180/180 ok · MAP paths 117/117 ok`. Only the store-budget class below remains. **Needs work (re-measured 2026-08-12 — four grew):** 8 store-budget violations — `.claude/rules/` bodies over the 600-word limit (`artifacts` 897, `chat-reducer` 789, `conversations` 848, `native-runtime` 3300, `react-renderer` 967, `registries` 605, `sync-spaces` 1213) and `docs/PITFALLS.md` at 2694/2500. **And the anchor class is red AGAIN (2026-08-12): `.claude/rules/sync-spaces.md` points at `youcoded/desktop/tests/sync-spaces-project-registry.test.ts`, which no longer exists** — see the fuller "youcoded-dev's OWN CI is red" entry under Bugs; these two entries are the same failure and should be worked together. Fix per `.claude/rules/README.md`: overflow migrates to the rule's lazy doc or becomes a pinning test — editorial trimming, not deletion. Context for whoever picks this up: the 2026-07-15 baseline report recorded this same command exiting **0** with `residue: 0`, so all 12 findings accrued in 15 days with nothing watching — which is precisely why the CI now exists. Landing it red was a deliberate call (Destin, 2026-07-30) so the failure is a forcing function, but a permanently-red check stops being read, so this should not sit indefinitely.

- [ ] Intermittent flake in `subagent-view.test.tsx`, `mcp-startup-wiring.test.ts`, and `sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts` `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-08-10; third file + root-cause lead added 2026-08-13)
  Pre-existing, seen across multiple unrelated branches while running `scripts/verify.sh` during the harness review-runner/tool-honesty work — load-correlated (fails under parallel-worker load, passes on an isolated re-run), same general shape as the other fixed-budget/shared-resource flakes already logged in this file (`harness-stall-watchdog`, `sync-spaces-engine`). Not yet root-caused for these two specific files. **That lead is already spent (noted 2026-08-12): `f05b2711` (2026-08-05, before this entry was filed) fixed the `SkillConfigStore` two-writer race that its own commit message blames for a prior `mcp-startup-wiring` failure — so whatever still flakes in these two files is something else.**
  **New file + a concrete lead (2026-08-13, found during the preparing-tool-cards work):** `tests/sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts > skips the file write when nothing changed` shows the same signature — ~1 failure in 3 full-suite runs, never once in 6 isolated runs. Measured on BOTH the feature branch and a clean `master` checkout (master failed 1 of 3), so it is not branch-specific. The failure is not an assertion but an exception: `ENOENT: no such file or directory, rename '<dir>/.claude/.sync-warnings.json.tmp.<pid>' -> '<dir>/.claude/.sync-warnings.json'`. **Verified mechanism:** `atomicWrite` in `src/main/sync-state.ts:223` builds its temp path as `target + '.tmp.' + process.pid` — *unique per process, not per call*. Two overlapping writes to the same target inside one worker therefore share one temp path, and the first `rename` consumes it out from under the second, which then ENOENTs. This is the identical shape `f05b2711` fixed in `SkillConfigStore`, whose `src/main/skill-config-store.ts:125` now appends `++tmpSeq` to the pid for exactly this reason — so the fix pattern already exists in the tree, one file over. **Not yet proven** that the test actually issues concurrent writes (the mechanism is confirmed; the trigger is not), so confirm that before changing `atomicWrite`. Note this is a *source* defect, not test-only: any two overlapping sync-state writes in the running app hit the same race.

## Features

- [x] Show progress while a tool call's arguments stream (the "silent Write" blind spot) `feature` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` `#ux` (added 2026-08-12, Destin; **SHIPPED 2026-08-13 — youcoded `0ccb576c`**)
  When a native model generates a large tool call — a Write whose input is a whole file — the app shows only the pulsing thinking indicator until the arguments finish. Reproduced live 2026-08-12 (dev session `c08e0b47`, DeepSeek v4-flash flappy-bird ask): ~4 min of reasoning, one line of visible text, then multi-minute silence while the Write args streamed; Destin read it as a hang, and reports this shape recurring. The stall watchdog is rightly quiet (data IS flowing) — the gap is presentational.
  **Verified feasible:** ai@7 emits `tool-input-start` / `tool-input-delta` / `tool-input-end` stream parts (present in `node_modules/ai/dist/index.d.ts`); the consume loop's switch (`harness-session.ts` ~1789) currently handles only the completed `tool-call` part, so the deltas are dropped on the floor today.
  **Shape to brainstorm, not decided:** the emit surface is FROZEN (`native-runtime.md`), so progress should ride the existing `assistant-thinking` heartbeat the way `promptProcessing` already does (display-only, never persisted) — e.g. `{ toolArgProgress: { toolName, chars } }` → ThinkingIndicator renders "Writing a file — 12 KB so far". Open questions: per-tool naming (Write has a file_path only after enough JSON has streamed to parse it), throttling (per-chunk emit would spam the IPC), and whether Bash/Edit want it too or only file-writing tools.
  **Update 2026-08-12 (same day):** Destin widened the goal — minimize generic-spinner time overall, preferring visible tool liveness or streaming text; an EARLY TOOL CARD (rendered at `tool-input-start`, detail filled as args stream) is now on the table alongside the progress line. Full investigation handoff with everything verified this session (adapter emission chain, watchdog feeding, OpenRouter probe result): `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-08-12-tool-streaming-visibility.md`. Probe preserved at `scripts/tool-arg-stream-probe.mjs`.
  **SPECCED 2026-08-12:** `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-12-tool-arg-streaming-visibility.md` — the early tool card won. A normal `ToolCard` appears at `tool-input-start` in its running state, detail line `preparing… N chars`, expanded body "Still preparing tool call…", and it transitions IN PLACE to the real card because `tool-input-start` and the completed `tool-call` carry the identical id (verified in the installed `provider-utils` dist) and `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_USE` is already idempotent by `toolUseId`. Progress rides `assistant-thinking` as `toolPreparing` (dropped by `session-store.ts:93`, so nothing partial is persisted); `preparing` is a boolean on a `running` entry, never a fifth `ToolCallStatus`; `endTurn` DELETES preparing cards rather than failing them. No partial-JSON parsing of `file_path` — dropped as a truncation-failure class for information the finished card shows moments later. Native-only; CC and Android unchanged.
  **SHIPPED 2026-08-13** (youcoded merge `0ccb576c`, plan `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-12-preparing-tool-cards.md`). Built as specced. Two things the plan did not foresee, both now in the code: (1) teaching `endTurn` to prune an emptied preparing group means it returns `assistantTurns`, which silently clobbered the two callers that build that map themselves — `TRANSCRIPT_TURN_COMPLETE` (usage/model stamping) and `TRANSCRIPT_INTERRUPT` (`stopReason`, whose "Interrupted" footer went blank) — so `endTurn` now takes their map as a third argument instead of spreading over it; (2) `injectPlanSegment` needs `beforeGroupId` now that placement runs first, or an ExitPlanMode plan renders *below* its own tool-group. Group placement for both the preparing and real paths is one shared helper (`placeToolInCurrentGroup`) precisely so they cannot drift apart — if they place differently, the real tool-use adds a second card instead of superseding. Verified live by Destin on a native session. Carried open item, not a blocker: whether llama.cpp's `--jinja` grammar-constrained path emits a first tool-call delta carrying the function name — a provider that never emits `tool-input-start` simply falls through to the old behavior.

- [ ] Tell the USER when context files were truncated, not just the model `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` `#ux` (added 2026-08-11, Destin)
  Every truncation the harness performs currently announces itself **to the model and nowhere else**. `fitProjectInstructions` outlines an over-budget `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` and appends a notice the model reads; `fitInjection` does the same for skill bodies, path-triggered rules, and nested instruction files; `mcpBudgetSizing` drops whole MCP servers from a session and records them in `HarnessSession.droppedMcpServers`, which **has no UI reader at all** (its own JSDoc says "a later task wires the actual surface"). So a user on a small local model can be running a session where most of their project's rules are outlined rather than present, several skills are cut, and entire MCP servers are unattached — with nothing on screen saying so. The failure is silent in exactly the way that produces "why is it ignoring my CLAUDE.md".
  **Wanted:** a session-start notice naming what was cut and by how much — the file(s), how much survived, and (for MCP) which servers were dropped. Destin's framing: make any context truncation transparent, with a message at the beginning of a session.
  **Design questions, not yet decided:** where it lives (a dismissible banner in the timeline, a status-bar chip, or an `(i)` on the existing context surface — the accessibility standard in `CLAUDE.md` argues for an explainer popup, not raw numbers); whether it fires per session or only when something actually got cut (the latter, or it becomes noise); whether it is per-file or one aggregate line; and whether it needs to survive resume (the notice is a fact about the session's prompt, which IS rebuilt on resume, so probably yes). Note the budget is fixed at session start and `setBinding` does NOT re-apply it — so switching to a smaller model mid-session does NOT re-truncate, and the notice must not imply otherwise.
  **Depends on nothing.** `fitProjectInstructions` already returns `{ text, truncated }` and `fitInjection` returns the same shape; both are currently discarded by their callers. `droppedMcpServers` is already tracked. This is a plumbing + UI task, not a new mechanism. Context on the truncation rework that surfaced it: youcoded PR #289 (project instructions are now outlined, not tail-cut).

- [ ] Move Write's and Edit's staleness checks from mtime to a content hash `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-10)
  Both tools currently compare `fs.statSync(abs).mtimeMs` against the mtime recorded at read time (`write.ts`, `edit.ts`) — deliberately made consistent with each other during the 2026-08-10 harness-tool-honesty work (Write previously had no freshness check at all; see `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-10-review-claims-verified.md` claim 2). mtime is the wrong long-term primitive: a `touch`/checkout can bump it with unchanged bytes (false-positive rejection), and some filesystems' clock resolution can miss a true same-second change (false-negative — a real external edit not caught). `docs/active/investigations/2026-08-10-harness-mutation-safety-prior-art.md` §1 documents two real precedents for the fix — Gemini CLI's Edit tool hashes with SHA-256 at read time and compares before applying; OpenCode V2's `FileMutation.writeIfUnchanged` does a raw byte-array comparison under a per-path mutex. Move BOTH tools together, not one at a time — an in-code WHY comment in `write.ts` already flags this as deferred-not-forgotten. Re-archive the mutation-safety doc once this ships.

- [x] **Workbench comparison view — 3 candidates side by side with round tracking** `feature` `#workbench` (added + shipped 2026-07-31, on `feat/session-launch-ux`)
  `?mode=workbench&view=compare`. Three candidate designs for one surface rendered simultaneously against the same mock backend, with a pick recorded per round so a design narrows over passes: pick one of three → Claude authors three more from it → pick again. Replaces the query-param switcher loop (`utils/design-variant.ts`, added and deleted three times), which meant flipping between candidates one at a time from memory AND editing the production component to hold them all. Candidates live in `dev/workbench/compare/registry.tsx` and are built from the REAL primitives and tokens — the only new code in a candidate is the arrangement under comparison, which is what makes a winner paste back into production unchanged. Two constraints found building it: a real `Dialog` can't be a candidate (fixed-position, all three would stack centre-screen — hence the `panel` frame), and picks live in localStorage because HMR reloads constantly.


- [x] Menu internals design system — the kit (K1–K12) + one dialog shell `feature` `#ui` (added 2026-07-26, **SHIPPED + MERGED 2026-07-28 — PR #264, 26 commits. All 12 roles + D1**)
  **Status 2026-07-28 (final) — THE KIT IS COMPLETE. All 12 roles + D1 shipped to `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1` (24 commits, UNMERGED, no PR). Destin reviewed and approved in a dev instance.** K5 and K9 were expected to need a fourth tranche behind a copy pass; the copy pass ran the same day and **turned out to be structural, not editorial** — seven of Remote Access's eleven setup branches carried over verbatim and all three danger zones already had a consequence sentence satisfying `docs/error-message-standards.md`. Five copy decisions (worksheet: `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-28-k5-k9-copy-pass.md`) and they landed in the same tranche. **One real bug fixed on the way:** `{setupError || 'Setup failed'}` replaced a missing reason with a hardcoded guess, leaving the user two words and no next step — now the real error + Retry when there is one, and the two-action fallback when there is not. That gave `ErrorState` its first call sites (it had none BY DECISION), so its exemption in `primitive-adoption.test.ts` came off and `error-message-standards.md` was corrected — it still told a future session to remove an entry that is now gone. **D1 chrome residue closed 2026-07-28:** six views (not the three first reported — that count came from memory rather than a search) still painted their own dialog header, close, back chevron and scroll body. Five converted; `SettingsPanel` and `SyncPanel` own their Dialogs so their views just pass `title`/`headerActions` up, and `ThemeScreen`'s `editingSlug` was lifted to its host the way K12 lifted `showInfo`. `SubViewHeader` deleted, three back-chevron reimplementations gone. **The setup wizard keeps its header by design** — its title derives from state that belongs inside it, and the real fix (the wizard owning its own `<Dialog>`) turns it from replacing the sync panel into stacking over it, which is a UX call. Guarded by `tests/dialog-chrome-authority.test.ts`. **Merged 2026-07-28 as PR #264** (26 commits, 3482 tests, all four CI jobs green). Docs archived to `docs/archive/`. Follow-ups tracked separately: the setup wizard owning its own `<Dialog>` (a UX call — it would stack over the sync panel rather than replace it), and the three counted backlogs the guards now hold. Three debts the guards surfaced are recorded as per-file COUNTS that may shrink and never grow — 9 hardcoded error fallbacks (that IS the v1.3.1 audit), 5 centred coloured status lines on non-settings surfaces, ~20 surviving `#DD4444` (change 17's unfinished business).

  **Status 2026-07-28 — tranches 1, 2/D1 and 3 are DONE and pushed to `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1` (23 commits, UNMERGED, no PR). Destin reviewed and approved in a dev instance.** Shipped: K1, K2, K3, K4, K6, K7, K8, K10, K12 and D1. **Remaining: K5 (status strip) and K9 (danger zone), both gated on a copy pass** — K5 turns Remote's eight setup branches into eight status messages and K9 needs a consequence sentence per danger zone, and `docs/error-message-standards.md` binds both, so those are Destin's words to write rather than Claude's to invent. K11 (footer) folded into K9: its only real dialog footer is ContextPopup's, and moving that warning out of the footer IS K9's "callout and control stay together" fix. Plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-07-28-menu-internals-tranche-3.md`. **The spec's ledger overstated what was left, for the fourth time (spec §0b holds the first three).** K12 was listed as "retires 5 mechanisms" but `SettingsExplainer` was already the one renderer with four hosts sharing one payload; the real remainder was that it predates `<Dialog>` and hand-rolled a header, a scroll body and an Esc handler. K11 had almost nothing independent left. K4 and K6 were undercounted in the other direction — K4 by two tones the spec cannot express (a `bg-green-500/10` success block, and `bg-red-500/10` at two border opacities around text already using the `destructive` token), K6 by two glyphs and by conflating three different problems under "the last bare ✕". Standing lesson, now proven twice: **read for the shape, not for the recipes someone already wrote down** — and that applies to the guard as hard as to the survey, since the first K4 guard required the tint before `border` and silently scored `Button.tsx` as zero.

  **Status 2026-07-26 — tranche 1 and tranche 2/D1 are DONE and pushed to `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1` (18 commits, UNMERGED, no PR).** Tranche 1: one section-label recipe (~90 sites, six orderings), decorative dividers deleted, the Sound bug, `SegmentedTabs` and `LoadingState` adoption. Tranche 2/D1: built `<Dialog>`, migrated **40 dialogs across 22 files**, deleted `SettingsPopup.tsx`. **Remaining in tranche 2: K2 rows** (decided — one component, two densities by role: `nav` `text-sm`/`2xs` for rows that navigate, `item` `text-xs`/`3xs` for rows being scanned; the five shapes K2 retires were about description PLACEMENT, not size, so the win survives). Then bookkeeping + PR, then tranche 3. **D1 needed three amendments once it met the code (spec §0b):** `SettingsPopup` was NOT "already correct" — it left the scroll body to callers and 2 of its 7 got it wrong, producing dialogs that clipped with no way to scroll; the `340/420/560/820` ladder was circular (fitted to arbitrary old values — `lg 560` was a width nothing had ever used) and was replaced by content-derived sizes `prompt 340 / panel 420 / document 600`, computed from reading measure now that the app is known to be entirely monospace (0.6em advance); and `max-h-[80vh]` was the wrong unit (≈700px on a laptop, ≈1730px on a 4K display) — the cap is now 1.4× each size's own width, clamped by `calc(100vh - 6rem)`. Two of my own errors are recorded there too, both the same shape (a negative asserted from a partial read, then written somewhere durable): `SyncPanel`/`QuickChips` exempted as "anchored popovers" when their style objects showed them centered, and an adoption guard scoped to `components/*.tsx` that could not see `App.tsx` or any subdirectory, hiding five more dialogs.

  **Update 2026-07-26 — approved and planned.** Destin approved the kit and chose **D2 = A** (drawer + modals): A is a strict subset of B, B is what C degrades to below 640px, and the kit is ~80% of the work in all three, so A costs nothing if B or C is wanted later. C declined — it re-opens change 50 (headerless drawer) and the 2026-07-23 git-branch popup spec, and its case doesn't clear that bar. Accepted cost of A, explicitly: 6 modal-on-modal sites and up to 2 stacked scrims remain. Tranche 1 plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-07-26-menu-internals-tranche-1.md` — five D2-independent tasks (K1 labels, K8 dividers, the Sound audition fix, K3 SegmentedTabs adoption, K10 LoadingState adoption), each with a source-text guard test in the established `*-authority.test.ts` style. Tranche 2 = D1 shell + K2/K6/K7 rows; tranche 3 = per-menu restructures (K4/K5/K9/K11/K12), gated on a copy pass. **Re-measurement against post-tranche-8 master corrected three numbers in the original draft** (spec §0): D4 type tokens **already shipped** in tranche 6 (`text-[10px]` 313→1) so the "prerequisite" tranche is done; `SegmentedTabs` now has 2 call sites, so the headline "zero call sites" finding is **stale** — though tranche 8's own audit replaced it with a stronger one (`FirstRunView` declared a LOCAL `ProgressBar` that *shadowed* the shared import, so grepping the name matched while the primitive had no real consumers; now guarded by `tests/primitive-adoption.test.ts`); and K1 is **78 sites, not ~30** — the original grep matched one Tailwind class ordering and missed a 49-site variant that renders identically. Standing rule this produced: audit a multi-class recipe on the *set* of classes, never the string.
  Spec: `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-26-menu-internals-design-system.md`. Artifact (interactive, 5 themes, verbatim class strings): https://claude.ai/code/artifact/7e886cfc-9957-4e5f-918d-3d83bd77e0a6. **Extends the 2026-07-16 UI-consistency spec, which locked *controls* across 8 tranches but never touched dialog anatomy or menu internals.** Destin's criterion (2026-07-25): *"clear/consistent menu structure across most menus (some unique elements are okay where they make sense for unique features, but elements performing similar functions should share design)"*. A first pass proposed three *shell* designs and was course-corrected — chrome discipline alone leaves every menu looking the same on the inside. The kit is 12 functional roles with one design each, measured against source: section label **4 shapes → 1**; setting row **5 → 1** (the single rule that kills all five: the description always lives in the left column under the title — Buddy's popup currently uses two different placements within itself); choice group **7 recipes → 3 with a decidable threshold rule** (4 radii, 4 text sizes, 3 inactive treatments today); callout 3 geometries → 1 shape/3 tones; status strip (Remote's 8 ad-hoc setup branches → 1); item list 3 → 1; field+action (kills the button-styled-as-a-field project-folder picker); divider **4 idioms → 0** (`space-y-5` + labels already separate); danger zone 3 bespoke → 1, callout and control kept together; empty/loading/error → the existing `ui/states.tsx` family, **which unblocks held change 33**; footer 3 conventions → 1 rule; explainer **5 mechanisms → 1** (all five already consume the same `{intro, sections}` payload). Four controls stay unique because the *function* is (theme tile grid, volume slider, QR pairing block, `kbd` chip) plus two inherited documented exceptions. Two findings that outlive whatever ships: **`ui/SegmentedTabs.tsx` has ZERO call sites** — built as change 45, never adopted, with seven hand-rolled choice groups sitting beside it (any primitive shipped without migrating its call sites in the same tranche lands here); and **`ProgressBar` + `UsageCard.tsx:79-80` already render context-remaining** while `ContextPopup.tsx:135` renders the same metric a second way as a `text-3xl` billboard. K1–K12 are shell-agnostic and can be approved independently. **Blocking decision is D2** — the navigation model: A drawer+modals (6 modal-on-modal sites, up to 2 stacked scrims, small migration) / B one frame with pages (0 stacked, +1 click to Remote→Devices, medium) / C screen+anchored popovers (0 stacked, largest migration, and it re-opens two shipped decisions — change 50's headerless drawer and the 2026-07-23 git-branch popup spec). They compose: A ⊂ B, and B is what C degrades to below 640px. D4 (type tokens, change 35, tranche 6) is a prerequisite — every recipe is written in `text-2xs/3xs/4xs`.
- [ ] Android toolchain migration: Kotlin 2.4 + AGP 9 + compose-bom 2026.06 `chore` `#android` `#build` (added 2026-07-25, from the dependabot triage — blocks 3 open PRs)
  Three youcoded dependabot PRs (#250, #237, #236) are **one coupled migration, not three bumps** — each fails on the next link in the chain, so they can only land together. Gradle 9.6.1 (#234) is already merged and was the first link; these are the rest. Failure modes read off the actual CI logs 2026-07-24/25:
  - **#237 AGP 8.7.0 → 9.3.1.** First failed with *"Minimum supported Gradle version is 9.5.0. Current version is 8.11.1"* — that's what merging #234 fixed. Rebased onto Gradle 9 it now fails **deeper**: *"An exception occurred applying plugin request [id: 'org.jetbrains.kotlin.android'] > Cannot add extension with name 'kotlin', as there is an extension already registered with that name."* AGP 9 registers its own `kotlin` extension, so it collides with the separately-applied Kotlin plugin at 2.1.0. **AGP 9 therefore requires the Kotlin bump in #244** — it cannot go in alone.
  - **#250 root gradle group (8 updates; superseded #244, which dependabot closed once #218 landed the safe subset).** Carries Kotlin 2.1.0 → 2.4.10, which fails at `app/build.gradle.kts:117`: *"Using 'jvmTarget: String' is an error. Please migrate to the compilerOptions DSL."* A real source change, not a version pin. (Its safe minor/patch subset — commons-compress, xz, zstd-jni, commonmark, Java-WebSocket, coroutines-test, mockito — already landed separately via #218, so #250 is now exactly the risky half — Kotlin 2.4.10, camera 1.6.1, activity/lifecycle — and still fails on the same jvmTarget line.)
  - **#236 compose-bom 2024.12.01 → 2026.06.01.** Fails `:app:lintVitalAnalyzeReleaseTest` with *"Unexpected failure during lint analysis of MainActivity.kt (this is a bug in lint or one of the libraries it depends on)"* — a ~19-month BOM jump against AGP 8's bundled lint. Plausibly resolves once AGP 9 brings a matching lint, which is another reason to sequence it with the other two rather than debug it alone.
  **Suggested order once someone picks this up:** migrate `app/build.gradle.kts:117` off the deprecated `jvmTarget: String` to the `compilerOptions` DSL first (it's the only hand-written change), then land Kotlin 2.4.10 + AGP 9.3.1 together, then re-run compose-bom. Android CI (`./gradlew test` + `assembleDebug` + `assembleReleaseTest`) is the gate and has been honest about every step so far.
  **Separately blocked on a compileSdk decision:** okhttp/mockwebserver 5.4.0 (#235, and the closed /app twins #219/#222) fail `checkDebugAarMetadata` — *"requires libraries and applications that depend on it to compile against version 36 or later of the Android APIs."* That's a compileSdk 36 bump, its own call, not part of this chain.
- [ ] Worker test-infra migration: vitest 4 + vitest-pool-workers 0.18 + wrangler 4 `chore` `#marketplace` `#worker` (added 2026-07-24, from the dependabot triage — blocks 3 open PRs)
  Three marketplace dependabot PRs (#60, #61, #63) all fail `npm ci` with ERESOLVE and **none of them can be merged as a dependency bump** — together they're one coordinated migration. Verified by building it in a scratch worktree 2026-07-24, not inferred from the peer ranges.
  **What's actually blocking.** #61 takes vitest 2.1.9 → 4.1.10 alone, but `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers@0.5.41` peer-wants `vitest 2.0.x - 2.1.x`. #60 takes pool-workers → 0.18.8, which peer-wants `vitest ^4.1.0`. So they're complementary halves — **but installing both together still doesn't work**: pool-workers 0.18.8 **deletes the `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers/config` subpath export entirely**, and `worker/vitest.config.ts:1` imports `defineWorkersConfig` from exactly that path. 0.18.8's root export is `cloudflarePool` / `cloudflareTest` / `readD1Migrations` — `defineWorkersConfig` no longer exists at all, so `npm test` dies at config load with `Missing "./config" specifier`. It also drags in `wrangler@4.114.0` transitively while `worker/package.json` pins `^3.80.0`, leaving two wranglers in the tree.
  **So the real unit of work is:** rewrite `worker/vitest.config.ts` to the vitest-4 pool API (the package ships a codemod at `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers/codemods/vitest-v3-to-v4`), bump wrangler 3 → 4, and re-verify the D1 migration harness (`readD1Migrations` + the `TEST_MIGRATIONS` binding + `environment: "test"`) still works. Doing the wrangler 4 bump here also unblocks **#63** (`@cloudflare/workers-types` 4 → 5, which fails because `wrangler@3.114.17` peerOptional-wants `^4.20250408.0`) — one migration clears all three PRs.
  **Why it deserves care rather than a quick bump:** `worker-deploy.yml` runs the test suite, then applies D1 migrations, then deploys to production on every push to master. A silently-degraded test harness doesn't just go red — it stops gating a live deploy.
- [ ] Resume-on-startup ("Welcome back") — rebuild on the Conversation Store `feature` `#conversations` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-24, from a branch audit — milestone TBD, Destin's call)
  **The want:** on cold start, if YouCoded had sessions live in the strip when it last shut down — window close, crash, OS kill, anything that wasn't an explicit per-session X — present a "Welcome back" screen listing them with per-session checkboxes and a one-click Resume-all, plus a Start-fresh escape. Originally specced 2026-05-01 (`docs/archive/specs/2026-05-01-resume-active-sessions-on-startup-design.md`); the UX in Task 6 of the archived plan is still the intended shape.
  **The 2026-05-01 attempt is dead and its architecture must NOT be revived.** `feat/resume-on-startup` (3 commits, Tasks 1–2 of 10, never PR'd, deleted 2026-07-24) persisted a second JSON file at `~/.claude/youcoded-active-sessions.json`. That predates the Conversation Store: master's records already carry a superset of its fields (`title`, `lastActive`, `projectName`, `originalPath`, `transcriptRef`, `device`, `lastUsedModel`) plus flags, convergent merges, healing, and sync. Reviving it means a second unsynced source of truth for conversation activity, and it's provider-blind — post-M2 a resume needs to know `'claude'` vs `'native'` and, for native, a model binding (`nativeHost.resume(id, cwd, binding)`). The archived plan carries a SUPERSEDED banner explaining this.
  **What's actually missing on master is small:** a durable *"this was open in the strip at shutdown"* marker. That's session-set membership, not conversation metadata, and `store-core.ts` already has `FlagState` + `setFlag`. Sketch: set the flag on first turn, clear it on explicit X-close, leave it alone on quit/crash; at startup list flagged records and hand them to the existing provider-aware resume path, which already handles leases, takeover, and the native model picker. Reuses `session-browser.ts`'s live-session exclusion rather than reinventing it.
  **Open design question that blocks it — device scoping.** Conversation records SYNC, so a naive boolean flag means the sessions left open on the Z13 pop up as a "Welcome back" screen on the Mac. Needs to key on the per-INSTALL `getDeviceIdentity(userData)` id — NOT the per-machine one (`docs/conversations.md` → leases, and the "Do NOT swap the lease id" invariant). Same family as the v1.3.1 cross-device native resume deferral; decide deliberately before building.
- [ ] No in-app way to resolve a sync conflict copy `feature` `#sync` (added 2026-07-24, Destin during the beta.9 dogfood — milestone TBD, his call)
  Today the entire conflict story is one transient amber line in the Backup & Sync panel: *"Some files had conflicting edits — the other device's copy was kept alongside yours (look for "(from …)" files)"* (`SyncPanel.tsx:1215`). It is keyed off a per-boot sync event (`visibleSpaceEvents.some(e => e.type === 'conflict')`), so it vanishes on restart whether or not anything was resolved; it names no file, offers no action, and sends the user to a file manager to hunt for `notes (from Laptop, 2026-07-03).md` — the copy `git-transport.ts` writes via `conflictCopyName` (`guards.ts:69`) when both devices edited one file.
  **Scope note:** conversation *records* do NOT need this — `foldConflictCopies` (`store-core.ts:256`) heals them automatically and the copies are pruned. What needs a resolver is user-visible FILES: synced project folders and Personal files. Whatever the UI is, it must (a) find copies by disk scan rather than a live event, so the state survives restarts and shows a real count; (b) offer per-file keep-mine / keep-theirs / keep-both with a preview for text; (c) route the resolution back through the sync engine as an ordinary synced change (delete/replace propagates), never a silent local edit; (d) stay honest about binary and large files, where a preview isn't possible.
- [x] Project View: merge Artifacts + All files into one Files tab `feature` `#project-view` (added 2026-07-23, **SHIPPED 2026-07-24 — youcoded PR #247, merge `b0f990b9`**)
  Destin's ask: the Artifacts tab looked like a subset of All files, so having both made no sense. **It wasn't a subset** — four things live only on the tracked side: external pins (`kind: 'external'`), version history (discovered files get `versions: []`, `project-file-discovery.ts:122`), deleted/orphan records, and gated roots (a home-dir project skips the disk scan entirely but still lists artifacts, `ipc-handlers.ts:3016`). So this is a merge, not a deletion.
  Shape: one `Files` tab (segments 4→3) = the on-disk `Project Files` walk only, in-folder artifacts **undifferentiated** (the disk is the truth). `+ Add file` becomes a native-picker **Move/Copy import** into the browsed folder (new `artifacts:import-file`; verify-before-unlink so a cross-filesystem move can't eat the source; Replace/Keep both/Skip asked once per batch, disclosed-collisions gate so an undisclosed collision falls back to keep-both; self-import guard so Move+Replace onto a file's own path can't delete it).
  **DESCOPED after Destin tested the branch (2026-07-23):** the design also added an `External Artifacts` section (sidecar records outside the folder, via a flipped `visible-artifacts.ts` rule 4 that dropped the pin requirement). Against his real sidecar it was ~95% incidental noise (163 scratchpad temps, 37 other-device Windows paths, 19 `.claude/` internals; `manualIncludes`=0). Removed; rule 4 reverted to "externals hidden unless pinned". Externals live in the Session Drawer per session — their correct home. The section was Claude's idea and it did not earn its place; the lesson is in the spec amendment banner.
  **"Show deleted" leaves Project View but STAYS in the session drawer** — `VersionEvent` has no content field (`shared/artifacts/types.ts:14`), so a deleted record is a tombstone, not a recovery path; but seeing everything Claude did in a session, deletions included, is the drawer's whole purpose. The flag is synced cross-device (`theme-context.tsx:526`), so a naive cleanup would drop a real preference — pinned by a guard test.
  **Desktop-only in practice:** every `artifacts:*` channel on Android is a `not-implemented-on-mobile` stub (`SessionService.kt:3585-3601`) — mobile Project View is v2, so the shared renderer shows the tab with no data behind it. Ships with an Android stub + a Move gate only. Rider, off the critical path: Android's picker renames every selection to `<timestamp>.<ext>` (`MainActivity.kt:56-64`), so chat attachments are all named `1784838134583.png` — fixed via `OpenableColumns.DISPLAY_NAME` in its own revertible commit.
  Spec: docs/active/specs/2026-07-23-project-view-file-merge-design.md · Plan: docs/active/plans/2026-07-23-project-view-file-merge-plan.md

- [ ] Session-switch motion — pill reveal + bubble pop `feature` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-20, **draft PR youcoded#192 — do NOT merge as-is**)
  Destin's ask: clicking between conversation tabs "feels jumpy/stuttery" — content pops in instantly and the switcher's tab names expand instantly instead of rolling out. His constraints: **no crossfade/slide** for bubbles (they should disappear and reappear *bouncy*), the strip should feel like the existing hover reveal over collapsed dots, and **only actually-visible bubbles animate**. Design approved visually first — mockup at `docs/active/prototypes/2026-07-20-session-switch-animation.html`.
  **Root cause worth keeping: the pill had TWO independent reasons it couldn't animate**, so "add a transition" was the wrong diagnosis and fixing either alone would have looked like the fix failed. (1) `maxWidth` was `undefined` when active (`SessionStrip.tsx:785-787`) — no numeric pair to interpolate. (2) `transition` was hard-set to `'none'` for every pack-expanded pill (`:789`), and `packSessions` puts the active pill in `expanded` unconditionally (`pack-sessions.ts:53`) — **the pill you just clicked was precisely the one with animation off.** Second finding: the "only visible bubbles" mechanism **already existed** — `ChatView.tsx:273-291`'s IntersectionObserver maintains `.in-view` for the glass optimization, so no new observer was needed.
  Implemented via `grid-template-columns: 0fr→1fr` (interpolates to *intrinsic* width, so the active pill keeps its uncapped shrink-and-ellipsis) + `bubble-switch-in/out` keyframes on `.timeline-entry.in-view` only. Panes stay mounted and stacked, so the outgoing one is held 120ms to finish its exit while incoming bubbles wait out an equal `animation-delay` — sequential, not the crossfade Destin rejected. Gated on `prefers-reduced-motion` + `reducedEffects` (the strip had no gate at all before). Desktop-only by nature: Android's `forceSingle` remounts its single always-named pill, so there's no before-state and no expansion to show.
  **Outstanding before merge** (full detail: `docs/active/handoffs/2026-07-20-session-switch-animation-handoff.md`): (1) the 120ms exit delays the incoming conversation and needs a lived-with verdict — deleting it is a one-line change; (2) it also fires on Ctrl+\` (chat↔terminal), because a ChatView's `sessionId` never changes so `visible` is the only available edge — suppressing needs a new signal from `App`; (3) **no test coverage** — nothing renders `SessionStrip` or `ChatView`, only `packSessions`' 9 pure cases are pinned, so decide whether the `.in-view`-only rule (the load-bearing perf property) earns a pinning test; (4) drag-reorder interaction with the `activeSwap` window untested; (5) narrow-viewport + remote unverified.

- [x] "Send Anyway" override on the pending-prompt send block `feature` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-16; shipped 2026-07-17, youcoded PR #165 merge `49bcf49b`)
  Carried out of the fixed "stale toast blocks send" bug (Destin's ask, recorded 2026-07-16): when the send gate claims Claude is waiting on a prompt, a human who can see there's no live prompt should be able to force the send rather than being locked out. Needs design care rather than a bare bypass — a forced send while an Ink menu IS live writes `text + \r` into the menu and can silently select an option; the affordance should probably interrupt/dismiss the pending state first or route through terminal view with a warning. **SHIPPED as ESC-first (Destin chose this over just-unlock):** a refused typed send now surfaces a "Send anyway" button on the toast (App `ToastState` gained an optional `{message, action}` form; string form preserved so the ~8 plain callers are untouched). Clicking it presses ESC in the PTY first — closes any genuinely-live Ink menu, harmless no-op on an idle bar, so it can NEVER answer a real menu — then re-sends past the gate via a `force` flag threaded through InputBar `send`/`sendMessage`. Scoped to the typed-message path (InputBar); programmatic sends (`/model`, `/sync`) keep the plain toast; the minimal touch bar (no handler) falls back to the plain toast. NOT unit-pinned — no InputBar test harness exists — typecheck-verified only; flagged to Destin for a visual eyeball on the next build. Known trade-off he accepted: clicking it while a REAL menu is up cancels that menu (the safe failure).

- [x] Chat view momentum ("flick") scrolling `feature` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-17, shipped 2026-07-17 youcoded PR #169 merge `f71c5f39`)
  Destin's ask: after flicking the trackpad, the chat should keep scrolling and decelerate at a reasonable rate; today it stops the instant the finger lifts. SHIPPED with a "tap to catch the glide" refinement (any touch while coasting freezes it in place, incl. sub-pixel resting-finger jitter). Verified in a dev instance. Root cause: the existing custom wheel handler (`ChatView.tsx:344-393`) hijacks native scroll via non-passive `preventDefault` + `scrollBy(deltaY * multiplier)` with burst-acceleration but NO inertia — and on Linux libinput emits no momentum tail (unlike macOS's ~20-30-event tail the handler's comment assumes), so scrolling dies on finger-lift. Fix = velocity-tracked `requestAnimationFrame` glide-out with friction deceleration, fed by the wheel deltas; cancel the glide on new wheel input / pointerdown / arrow-key scroll / unmount, and stop at scroll boundaries. Gate momentum to trackpad pixel-mode (`deltaMode===0`) so discrete mouse-wheel stays snappy. Shared renderer code but inert on Android (no wheel events on touch) — must not disturb the atBottom auto-scroll effects (208-266) or `scrollToBottom`. Feel is timing-sensitive → tune constants then hand Destin a dev instance to eyeball the deceleration rate.
- [x] Right-click context menu (copy/paste/context-aware + Ask about this + file-pill menu) `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` (added 2026-07-17, shipped 2026-07-17 youcoded PR #169 merge `f71c5f39` — image sub-menu split to its own item below)
  Destin's ask: standard right-click behavior for copy/paste "and other standard left-click shit." Today there is NO context menu anywhere in the app (no `onContextMenu`, no Electron `Menu`, no `contextmenu` listener — verified 2026-07-17); right-click does nothing. Decision (Destin, 2026-07-17): CUSTOM THEMED React menu (not native Electron menu) — app fonts/colors/glass blur, follows active theme + wallpaper, cross-platform (desktop + Android long-press + remote browser). Built on the app's real `.layer-surface` popover, styled like `components/ui/Select.tsx` option rows (text-2xs, text-fg-2, hover bg-inset, accent-icon primary items). Context-AWARE scope per target: selected read-only text → Ask about this · Copy · Select all; code block → Ask about this · Copy code block · Copy · Select all; link → Open link · Copy link address · Copy · Select all; image → Ask about this · Copy image · Save image as… · Copy image address; editable input bar → Cut · Copy · Paste · Select all (Cut/Copy/Paste/Select-All disabled-not-hidden when unavailable). Reuse existing primitives: `navigator.clipboard`, `IPC.CLIPBOARD_SAVE_IMAGE`, `window.claude.shell.showItemInFolder`/`openPath` (preload.ts:677/681). **"Ask about this"** (text/code/image): drop the target into the input bar with a follow-up scaffold ("In an earlier message, you said: \"…\".\n\nThe user has a follow-up: ") and park the cursor — simple v1 = plain prompt text, no new plumbing (see separate roadmap item for the native version). **File-pill menu** (NEW, Destin 2026-07-17): right-click on the file pills/buttons in chat tool cards (`tool-views/ToolBody.tsx:151`, which already OPEN on left-click) → Open file · View in folder · Copy file name · Copy as path. Kept on right-click to preserve left-click open; confirm with Destin if he wants it on left-click instead. Mockup approved (artifact 2a6ebbe2 rev 2; sparkle icon chosen for Ask-about-this). **IMPLEMENTED (pending Destin's dev review), branch `feat/chat-flick-scroll`:** new `components/context-menu/` module — `ContextMenuHost` (one document-level capture `contextmenu` listener, mounted in App.tsx by RemoteSnapshotExporter), `build-menu.ts` (pure DOM/selection → entries, scoped to `.chat-scroll` + `.input-bar-textarea` — later extended to `[data-artifact-viewer]` + `.artifact-edit-textarea`, see the artifact-viewer item below), `ContextMenu.tsx` (portalled `.layer-surface` L4 popover, edge-flip, arrow-key nav, Esc/click-outside/scroll dismiss), `menu-icons.tsx`, `clipboard.ts` (secure-context writeText + execCommand fallback for insecure remote). Ask-about-this reuses a new `youcoded:compose-insert` window CustomEvent (InputBar prepends scaffold + caret). File pills got `data-file-path` (absolute `fp`) in ToolBody.tsx; desktop-gated Open/Reveal via existing shell IPC. **DEFERRED — image sub-menu (Copy image / Save image / Copy address):** chat/artifact images are ephemeral `blob:` URLs and there is NO copy-image-to-clipboard or save-image-to-disk IPC (only `clipboard:save-image` which READS the OS clipboard). Doing it right needs 2 new main IPC handlers + bytes plumbing (`artifacts.readBinary`) + `data-artifact-path` on ImageView/ArtifactThumbnail imgs + 3-surface parity + won't work on Android/remote — split out below. Typecheck clean; not unit-pinned (no host for these renderer interactions) — verified by dev review.
- [x] Agent-shell Bash CWD — scoped persistence (Claude Code's model) `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-07-17, shipped 2026-07-18 — youcoded PR #174)
  Today every Bash tool call is a **fresh process** started at the session cwd: `cd` in one call does NOT carry to the next, and nothing tells the model that — verified 2026-07-17 (PID changed `1042732 → 1042910` across two calls). Observed cost in one session: ~6 failed tool calls (`node_modules/.bin/vitest` "not found", `git add` "pathspec did not match") before the model diagnosed that CWD simply never persists. This is a known, contested design point in agentic harnesses — Claude Code issues #42837, #35058, #28228, #22023, #37659 all debate it, and the #1 complaint across them is *silent* behavior (the model can't tell which cwd model it's in and guesses wrong). Three models in the wild: persistent process (Anthropic API Bash tool), scoped persistence (Claude Code CLI default — cwd persists only within approved dirs, `cd` outside reverted with a `Shell cwd was reset to …` notice), and stateless/always-reset (many CI harnesses).
